## Session start

_(2026-10-25 — fourteenth cut-improvement session, written before any cut-file edit, per
IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md's mandatory gate. The instruction is generic again ("Do IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md
for 1 cut, then do CHORES.md") with no cut named, so the cut is chosen by the scoring system's own
rule: sort all cut files by `quality_score` ascending and take the lowest. The previous pass
finished `baltic_herring` at 19/20 with its three user-approved exceptions, so the queue's bottom
has moved to a different cut; I will enumerate the frontmatter scores across the recipe tree after
this gate, pick the unique lowest (documenting any tie-break), and run the full sub-step sequence
on it — (a) cross-tradition method research with real fetched sources, (b) create or improve child
`{cut}-{method}.md` files toward an average of 4 per cut, (c) update the `methods:` list — then an
honest from-criteria rescore, a commit, and only then CHORES.md.)_

### Output 1 — Culinary group definitions (verbatim from `RECIPE_COLLECTION_TOR.md`)

| Culinary Group | Includes |
|-----------|---------|
| **Culinary Group A** | South Asian, Southeast Asian, East Asian, Mongolian (e.g. Japanese, Chinese, Korean, Thai, Vietnamese, Bengali, Ceylon, Indian, Indonesian, Filipino) |
| **Culinary Group B** | North American, Western/Southern/Central European, Australasian (e.g. American, British, French, German, Italian, Spanish, Australian, Greek) |
| **Culinary Group C** | African, Caribbean (with African diaspora influence), Arabic, Levant, Persian/Iranian, non-western Pacific Islands (e.g. Nigerian, Ethiopian, Moroccan, Egyptian, Lebanese, Caribbean, Creole, Turkish) |
| **Culinary Group D** | Scandinavian, Baltic, Slavic, Estern europe, Russia, Ukraine, Caucasus region, Kazakstan, Siberia, Sami, Inuit (e.g. Swedish, Norwegian, Danish, Finnish, Icelandic, Russian, Polish, Georgian, Latvian, Lithuanian, Estonian, Sami, Inuit) |

### Output 2 — Analysis gate rule (verbatim from `RECIPE_ANALYSIS_TOR.md`)

> Analysis begins only after the collection phase (4–6 fully saved source recipes per leaf) is complete. Drawing conclusions from a leaf with incomplete source recipes produces invalid data.

### Output 3 — Source integrity rule (verbatim from `SOURCE_SURVEY.md`)

> The rule in this project is: **do not write content you cannot cite to a real URL or a real published book**. Training-data recall of "what a recipe roughly contains" is not an acceptable source. Use web_fetch to verify you can actually read the content.

### Output 4 — My understanding of this file (own words)

The primary task is to thoroughly improve the lowest-scoring cut file with real, fetched, citable
research: actively discover the cooking methods this cut is actually cooked with across culinary
traditions rather than trusting its frontmatter, create or improve a child `{cut}-{method}.md` leaf
for every confirmed method toward an average of four per cut (each leaf built from 4–6 fully saved,
readable source recipes — never from memory), update the `methods:` list to match, and then rescore
the cut from the written criteria rather than from the previous number. "This is not a race" means
quality and honest data outrank speed and score: spending a lot of time and tokens on real research
is correct and expected, a small or zero score movement is a fine outcome, a hard file is never
abandoned or guessed through, and a fabricated name or temperature corrupts the whole scoring
system — partial-but-honest always beats complete-but-invented, and full score is not the goal.
After this one cut is fully worked, committed, and rescored — not before — I do CHORES.md
(version bump per the scheme, branch history entry, user guide + GUI help anchors).

## Cut-improvement session (`baltic_herring`) + CHORES — completed 2026-10-25 (thirteenth pass) — v0.10.2.19

Per the session-start gate above: the queue's unique lowest scorer, `baltic_herring` (frontmatter
6/20), run through the full sub-step sequence and then CHORES.md. Two Cloud runner restarts hit
mid-session; the worktree survived both and work resumed without redoing completed steps.

### 1. Method discovery — sixth method `braise`

The eastern-Baltic search (RU/LV/EE/PL sources instead of more Swedish duplication) found
braising/stewing in sauce: Povar.ru *Салака тушеная* (20+5 min covered), iamcook.ru *Тушеная
салака* (~10 min, "do not stir"), nra.lv *Krējuma mērcē sautētas reņģes* (200 °C oven, cream).
New leaf `baltic_herring-braise.md`; `CookingMethod.BRAISE` registered in `cooking_data.py` and
`braise` added to the cut's `methods:` list. This supersedes the 2026-10-24 "none beyond the
five" finding — that pass had not searched the RU/LV/EE naming pool. All six methods now have
leaves; the cut file's method-survey paragraph records this.

### 2. Leaf widening — to the 4–6 window, no new Swedish sources

| Leaf | Before | After | Added 2026-10-25 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pan_fry` | 5 | **6** | PL AniaGotuje.pl; three EE/LV/RU fetched as leads — window full |
| `oven_bake` | 3 | **6** | EE Tuuli Retseptid, LV Pie Galda, RU Povar.ru |
| `grill` | 4 | **6** | EE Balti Jaama Turg, RU EdimDoma |
| `boil` | 4 | **6** | EE Kalanduse teabeskuskus, LV nra.lv |
| `smoker` | 2 | **4** | RU Perekrestok, EE Pühajärve Kalapäev |
| `braise` | — | **3** | RU ×2 + LV ×1 (one short; DIGAR lead uncollected) |

All six leaves **fail** the distinct-tradition gate exactly as written (SE ×2, SE ×2, SE ×3,
FI ×3, SE ×2, RU ×2) and keep their `⚠️ PARTIAL` markers — the gate is a gate (passed or not
passed), never "mostly passed". Widening used only EE/LV/PL/RU sources: no further Swedish
recipes were added.

### 3. First cut-specific temperature evidence — 52 °C

Both Swedish guides fetched and verified verbatim (Pattern 8): Kokaihop — *"52 grader är den
perfekta temperaturen för fisk … strömming …"*; Kungsberga fish table — *52 °C gäller för …
Sill/Strömming …* with the pull-2–4 °C-earlier rule (matches `carryover_temp_c: 2`). Written into
the cut profile (EN + SV) with the provenance caveat: Kungsberga compiles partly from the same
kokaihop.se tillagningstemperaturer list — one Swedish lineage, not two. Still stated: no source
**recipe** states a probe temperature, and 52 °C sits below the generic 63 °C safety line —
both recorded, neither merged.

### 4. Rescore — 6 → 19 with the user-approved exceptions #1–#3

From-scratch row-by-row rescore (Patterns 4/8/9/10): preferred-temps (−4 → 0) and per-method
(−2 → 0) **earned** by fetched evidence; source count improved to a single −1 (`braise` — agent
work, never exception-eligible). The user approved three hard-wall exceptions per the TOR, each
recorded with wall evidence and auto-revocation conditions: **#1 identity** (Groups A and C),
**#2 external-authority** (USDA has no species entry for strömming), **#3 corpus-saturation**
(distinct-tradition gate on all six leaves — clearing it would need heartland sources deleted).
**Score: 19 / 20** with exceptions; **10 / 20** raw (−1 braise, −6 gate, −2 A/C, −1 USDA).
Frontmatter `quality_score: 19`, `quality_assessed: 2026-10-25`.

### 6. Same-day continuation (`pan_fry`, `oven_bake`) — score 6 → 8

After the CHORES commit the user said "continue", so the two leaves that were exactly one tradition
short of the gate were finished off. Both needed the *missing culinary group*, not merely another
source, which is why the searches were aimed at a group rather than a method:

| Leaf | Was | Now | Source added | Group closed |
|------|----:|----:|--------------|--------------|
| `pan_fry` | 3 (SE, JP, TR) | **4 — ✅ CONFORMS** | James Morton's *Oatmeal-Fried Mackerel* (The Wordrobe, from the cookbook *Shetland*), Scottish, full quantities, "a couple of minutes on each side" | B |
| `oven_bake` | 3 (JP, UK, TR) | **4 — ✅ CONFORMS** | Anna Wendt, Allt om Mat (Expressen), *Ugnsbakad makrill med ärtkräm* — 175 °C, 15 min, **"tills fiskens innertemperatur är 52°"** | D |

The Swedish source is the most consequential find of the pass: **a measured 52 °C internal
endpoint**, 11 °C below the USDA fish minimum and below every band the cut defines. It was *not*
adopted — the leaf records it, the consensus section explains why it cannot map to a doneness key,
and the cut file's new priority 5 states what evidence would be needed before offering a soft-bake
option (a second corroborating sub-63 °C source, then an explicit product decision). Reinstating the
retired `medium` band (57 °C) was considered and rejected: this source is lower still.

Rejections recorded rather than papered over: **BBC Good Food's pan-fried mackerel with orange
salsa** now returns only subscriber boilerplate, so it is a failed fetch; the Portuguese-language
results for pan-fried mackerel were overwhelmingly *horse* mackerel (carapau/chicharro, genus
*Trachurus*), a different fish, and were skipped on species grounds rather than counted.

Score −14 → **−12** (partial leaves 14 → 12), i.e. **8 / 20**. Verification after the change:
29/29 tests OK, `py_compile` clean, generator clean (`PANEL_VERSION` 655), 4 conformant leaves and
12 partial ones, header score and table score both 8.

**Remaining for `mackerel`** (recorded in its Improvement priorities): 12 leaves are still partial;
thinnest are `steam` (+3), `saute` (+3), `pan_sear` (+3), `smoker` (+2), `simmer` (+2), then
`oven_broil` (+1). `braise` needs a **fourth tradition**, not another source — its Korean duplicate
blocks conformance, so the cheapest route is a Chinese red-braised or Japanese *nimono* mackerel
primary. The cut now has **two** measured endpoints, and they point in opposite directions: 63 °C
(`smoker`, `air_fryer`) and 52 °C (`oven_bake`); every other leaf states published absences instead of
inferring figures. Nothing was pushed.

## Cut-improvement session (`baltic_herring`) + CHORES — completed 2026-10-24 (seventh pass) — v0.10.2.14

Per the user's instruction: IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md for **one** cut, then CHORES.md. The mandatory
`## Session start` gate (four outputs, verbatim) is at the top of this file and was written before
any cut file was opened. The lowest-scoring cut in the tree was chosen: **`baltic_herring` at 3/20**
(the only cut below 4 besides `mackerel` at 4, which a prior pass had just improved).

### 1. Method research (sub-step a)

The 2026-10-23 survey (Matkult.se archival inventory: stekning, ugnsbakning, rökning,
glödstekt/halstrad, kokning) was **re-verified with fresh searches**, including deliberate checks
for poach/simmer candidates: Swedish "sill i dillsås" is a pickled-product preparation (Orkla
product listings, not a thermal method for fresh strömming), and Japanese *nishin* preparations
(simmered, grilled) use Pacific herring, a different species. **No sixth thermal method was found**;
`cooking_methods_researched: 1` stands, and `cooking_data.py`'s five supported methods
(PAN_FRY, OVEN_BAKE, SMOKER, GRILL, BOIL) needed no change.

### 2. Leaves rebuilt from fetched sources only (sub-step b)

Eight new sources added across three leaves; every URL was fetched and readable, every recipe
records its published absences instead of inferred temperatures (SOURCE_SURVEY.md rule held):

| Leaf | Before | After | New sources |
|------|-------:|------:|-------------|
| `pan_fry` | 3 | **5** | Fishbox/Delia Online *Herring in Oatmeal* (Scottish, Group B); Lecker.de *Gebratene Heringe* (DE, Group B) |
| `grill` | 1 | **4** | Köket.se *Sotig strömming på grillen* (SE, 3–4 min/side); Lisa Elmqvist *Halstrad strömming* (SE, 4–5 min/side over embers); What's Up Yukon *Rosemary grilled herring* (CA, Group B) |
| `boil` | 1 | **4** | Martat.fi *Kirkas silakkakeitto* (FI); Isoäidin reseptillä *Silakkakeitto* (1909 Otava cookbook reproduction, FI); Pro Kala *Silakka-pastakeitto* (FI) |

Species honesty: the Scottish, German and Canadian sources say *herring* (Atlantic) without naming
the Baltic subspecies — each is marked as such in its leaf rather than passed off as strömming.
The German *Brathering* (pan-fry-then-pickle) tradition is recorded as tradition context in
`pan_fry`, not as a source recipe, because its pages document the technique without a quantified
frying step. Two fetch failures are recorded rather than faked: germanfoods.org (no readable text)
and Livsmedelsverket's consumer page (403) — the latter's guidance was obtained from the fetched
Kontrollwiki page instead.

**Groups A and C searched and kept missing**: Caribbean herring traditions use the *smoked*
product (choka), and East Asian herring preparations use Pacific herring — neither is a fresh
strömming cooking tradition, so the −1 deductions stand with the searches documented (this is
Pattern 3/Pattern 10 discipline: the deduction survives a real search instead of an assumption).

### 3. Honest rescore: 3 → 6 / 20

All deductions re-checked against evidence (HOW_I_INFLATE Patterns 4 and 8). Source-count
deduction −5 → −2 (only oven_bake 3 and smoker 2 remain below 4); **Group B recovered** (−1 → 0);
safe-temp −3 → −1 because generic fish-safety guidance is now fetched (FSA 63 °C via Thermapen UK;
Livsmedelsverket ~70 °C core guidance via Kontrollwiki; Köket.se fish internal-temp table fetched
but has no herring row) while **no strömming source states any internal temperature** — the
preferred-temp (−4) and per-method (−2) deductions stand untouched. New −3 row for the
distinct-tradition gate: the three improved leaves meet the 4–6 count but each fails tradition
spread (SE ×2, SE ×3, FI ×3), so they keep their `⚠️ PARTIAL RESEARCH` markers and are **not**
counted as conformant. All three leaves' temperature tables state the full observed time ranges
(2–6 min/side pan-fry, 3–5 min/side grill) while recording that no source publishes a pull temp.

### 4. CHORES.md — the three tasks

## Isle of Man cuisine data + CHORES — completed 2026-10-24 (sixth pass) — v0.10.2.13

This pass added the researched Isle of Man (Manx) cuisine file the maintainer asked for, then ran
the three CHORES.md tasks.

### 1. New cuisine file — `www/cuisines/isle_of_man.md`

**104 ingredients across all 15 category-grade pairs** (Proteins 8/8/8, Produce 8/9/8, Grains &
Starches 6/6/6, Dairy/Oils/Sauces 8/8/2, Spices/Nuts/Seasonings 8/8/3 — signature/bulk/local),
researched against IMPROVE_CUISINE_DATA.md and validated by the cuisine catalogue tests.

**Research basis.** Primary historical source is *The Manx Cookery Book of Favourite Dishes*
(Morrison, 1908), with Manx Gaelic food vocabulary from Culture Vannin (skeddan, praas, loaghtan,
bainney, sollan, pibbyr …). Modern evidence: gov.im fisheries and agriculture pages, Isle of Man
Creamery (est. 1934, 28 family farms, 26M+ litres of milk a year), Laxey Glen Mills, Isle of Man
Salt Co. (hand-harvested sea salt, founded 2021), Isle of Man Farmers' Markets, Bangor University
Annual Fisheries Science Report 2023 (1,816 t king scallops landed 2022/23), the GOV.UK protected
food register (Isle of Man Manx Loaghtan Lamb PDO, EU registration 3 April 2008), RBST breed
register, and BBC Isle of Man 2025 café-culture material.

**Signature anchors:** herring (skeddan — spuds an' herrin', the national dish) and queen scallops
(queenies) at 9, Manx Loaghtan lamb PDO at 8, potatoes at 9 (~3,500 t grown a year, the island's
biggest food crop). **Local anchors:** king scallop at 9, whelk, mackerel, limpets (flitters) and
winkles from the shore, rainbow trout from the Government's Cornaa hatchery (20,000 stocked a year).

**Two facts recorded in the file's Research notes because they shape every pair:** (1) no Isle of Man
food-consumption statistics exist anywhere (gov.im publishes agriculture/fisheries only; DEFRA
*Family Food* has no Isle of Man sample), so every Bulk item is an explicitly labelled **UK proxy**
with its source named — not an Isle of Man measurement; (2) Manx produce is only about **6%** of food
consumed on the island (Manx NFU), so Local pairs are the genuine minority.

**Two pairs documented as genuinely insufficient after three search angles each**, per the floor
rule: Dairy/Oils/Sauces-Local (2 verified items — Islander Red cheese, salted caramel sauce) and
Spices/Nuts/Seasonings-Local (3 — Manx sea salt, smoked sea salt, herb sea salt; the local
sub-variety exception applied to salt once, sparingly). These carry `<!-- Searched: … -->` comments
so the next agent knows they were attempted, not skipped.

**One ingredient deliberately omitted:** the 1908 *Bollan ny Houney* (Hollantide Eve supper) names
its fish only as "bollan" and no source read confirms the species — recorded as unresolved in the
Research notes rather than guessed.

**Sanity:** the generator loads the file as cuisine **#103** (12,863 ingredients total, +104);
`test_canonical_cuisine_metadata_has_danish_parity`, `test_generator_preserves_danish_cuisine_metadata`
and the other catalogue tests pass with it in place (it carries `name_da`/`description_da` like every
canonical file).

### 2. CHORES.md — the three tasks

1. **Version:** `0.10.2.12` → **`0.10.2.13`** in `manifest.json`, `__init__.py` (Last Change header, the
   version comment and `__version__`) and the `const.py` Last Change header. `generate_frontend_data.py`
   ran twice this pass (once after the cuisine file, once after the bump): 103 cuisines, 12,863
   ingredients, 694 recipe files, 188 cuts, 186 EXP_TREE cuts, `PANEL_VERSION` **647 → 649**, Android
   assets refreshed.
2. **Branch history:** this entry. Also recorded here because it changes the branch state: the work
   through **`0d315004`** (the v0.10.2.11 and v0.10.2.12 passes below) has **landed on GitHub `main`** —
   `git fetch` on 2026-10-24 resolves `main` to `0d315004`, identical to `HEAD`, so the branch is now
   **0 ahead, 0 behind** and the merge-readiness rows in the two entries below (“12 ahead”, “180 files
   differing”) describe the pre-push state. Nothing was pushed by this pass.
3. **User guide:** the label is **`0.10.2.13`**. The Isle of Man addition needed no other guide change:
   §7.2 describes the cuisine filter generically (“Over 100 cuisines are available” — still true at
   103) and the guide never enumerates cuisine files. **Help anchors:** all **14**
   `_openHelp('#…')` anchors re-resolved against the guide's headings — **14/14**, no heading was
   renamed, so no anchor needed updating. The guide's **79** internal cross-links: **79/79** resolve.

## Problem fixes + CHORES + merge preparation — completed 2026-10-24 (fifth pass) — v0.10.2.12

This pass fixed the defects the v0.10.2.11 entry recorded as "found but not fixed", re-ran the
CHORES.md tasks and repeated the merge checks. It also swept for the *same classes* of defect
elsewhere, which is where three of the numbers below come from.

### 1. The AI cooking-style map was stale (`MEATER_METHOD_TO_AI_STYLE`)

The map in `panel-class-template.js` had **13 keys, five of them for slugs that do not exist**
(`slow_roast`, `bbq`, `smoke`, `deep_fry`, `air_fry` — the last two are *appliance feature* names, not
method slugs) and was missing **11 live methods**. Only 8 of the 19 method buttons a user can press
resolved to a deliberate style; the rest fell through to `comfort_food`.

It is now one entry per live method, and the AI Recipe Builder is seeded with the intended style:
Oven Roast / Oven Bake / Whole Roast → Comfort Food · Grill / Charcoal Grill / Smoker → Barbeque ·
Pan Sear / Pan Fry / Sauté / Air Fryer / Oven Broil → Quick And Easy · Slow Cooker / Braise → Slow Cook ·
Sous Vide → Gourmet · Steam / Poach → Healthy · Simmer / Boil / Pressure Cooker → One Pot.

Guarded by a new test (`test_meater_method_style_map_covers_live_methods`): the map must cover every
live `methods:` slug, must not contain a slug that is not live, and every target id must exist in
`COOKING_STYLES`. The guide's “Get AI Recipe Suggestions” step now shows the full mapping table
instead of the old one-line example.

### 2. 46 dead method links in 13 cut files

Every white-fish cut below declared methods in `methods:` and listed them as links to leaf files that
were never written:

| Cut | Dead links | Cut | Dead links |
|-----|-----------:|-----|-----------:|
| `arctic_char` | 4 | `perch` | 3 |
| `burbot` | 3 | `pike` | 4 |
| `flounder` | 4 | `plaice` | 4 |
| `gilt_head_bream` | 4 | `pollock` | 4 |
| `monkfish` | 4 | `redfish` | 4 |
| `sole` | 3 | `turbot` | 2 |
| `whitefish` | 3 | | |

Each link is now plain text — `- Grill — no research file yet` — while the method stays declared, so
the panel still offers it and the overview no longer points at nothing. A full relative-link sweep of
the recipe tree now reports **0 broken links**. The guide's cut-overview template shows the convention.

### 3. Doneness recommendation flags: 23 contradictions, 36 omissions, 1 double flag

The previous entry left 23 cuts whose `recommended: true` sat on a different band than their
`recommended_doneness`. Sweeping every cut showed the flag was unreliable in three ways, all now
fixed (73 cut files touched in total, 60 by this item):

| Problem | Count | Fix |
|---------|------:|-----|
| Flag on a band other than `recommended_doneness` | 23 | Flag moved to the recommended band (e.g. `mahi_mahi` medium → well_done, `pork_chop` medium → well_done, `scallops` medium_rare → well_done) |
| No flag anywhere although `recommended_doneness` is set | 36 | Flag added to the recommended band (`brisket`, `lamb_shank`, `pork_shoulder`, `beef_liver`, …) |
| Two flags, neither on the recommended band | 1 | `tuna_steak`: rare + medium unflagged, `medium_rare` (its recommended level) flagged |

**This changes no behaviour and no temperature.** The generator emits only `recommended_doneness`;
the panel's ⭐ and its preselected button are derived from that field, never from the per-band flag —
so a cut whose flag was on the wrong band already showed the star on the right one. What was wrong was
the data's own documentation, which the guide tells contributors to keep to "exactly one entry".

A new test (`test_recommended_doneness_carries_the_recommended_flag`) holds the invariant: the
recommended band must be flagged, and any *other* flagged band must be `usda_safe: true` — which is
exactly the 14 poultry and goose files that legitimately carry both `safe` and an `…_optimal` /
`…_rendered` band.

### 4. Two dead links in `README.md`

Both `Current Status` sections pointed at `STATUS.md`, which no longer exists in the repository. The
dangling references were removed; the surrounding status text is unchanged.

### 5. CHORES

1. **Version:** `0.10.2.11` → **`0.10.2.12`** in `manifest.json`, `__init__.py` (Last Change header, the
   version comment and `__version__`) and the `const.py` Last Change header. `generate_frontend_data.py`
   ran clean: 694 recipe files, 188 cuts, 186 EXP_TREE cuts, 102 cuisines, `PANEL_VERSION` **646 → 647**,
   Android assets refreshed.
2. **Branch history:** this entry, plus the v0.10.2.11 entry below, which keeps its numbers.
3. **User guide:** the label is `0.10.2.12`; §5.2 states that a multi-level cut preselects and stars its
   preferred level; §5.9's AI-style step carries the full method → style table; §5.10 documents the
   `recommended: true` invariant (flag = `recommended_doneness` band, extra flags only on `usda_safe`
   bands) and the "no research file yet" convention for unresearched methods.
   **Help anchors:** all **14** `_openHelp('#…')` anchors re-resolved against the guide's headings —
   **14/14**; no heading was renamed. The guide's **79** internal cross-links were also checked: **79/79**
   resolve.

## CHORES + merge preparation — completed 2026-10-24 (fourth pass) — v0.10.2.11

This pass ran the three CHORES.md tasks and prepared the branch for merge. It also found and fixed
the one thing that actually blocked merging: the branch's own test suite was red, and had been since
the grayling pass on 2026-10-23.

### 1. Merge blocker — the test suite was failing before this pass

`python3 -m unittest discover -s tests` on the untouched tree at `0e47f0dc`: **27 tests, 1 failure** —
`test_catalog_localization.test_all_live_cooking_methods_are_translated` asserted that 17 cooking
methods exist across all cut files and found **19**. The branch had registered `poach` (grayling,
`514475be`, 2026-10-23) and `simmer` at some point after that; `main` has neither, so this was
introduced here, not inherited. The same test's second half — every language translating every live
method — was never reached because the count assertion failed first.

Nothing in this repository was lying about it: the panel's method buttons resolve
`cooking_methods.<slug>` and fall back to the de-slugged slug when there is no entry, so `Poach` and
`Simmer` rendered as English words inside the Svenska and Dansk interfaces.

Fixed in three places, so the guard keeps its teeth rather than being relaxed:

| File | Change |
|------|--------|
| `i18n/en.json`, `i18n/sv.json`, `i18n/da.json` | `poach` → Poach / Pochera / Pochering, `simmer` → Simmer / Sjuda / Simring, inserted in the panel's display order after `steam`. `cooking_methods` is 17 → 19 in each file |
| `tests/test_catalog_localization.py` | expected count 17 → 19, with a comment naming the two methods and the requirement, so the assertion stays a guard against an *untranslated* method reaching the panel |
| `www/kitchen-cooking-panel.js` | regenerated — it bakes `I18N_STRINGS` from the three JSON files, and one test compares the two sides exactly |

After the regeneration: **27 tests, OK**. The full live method set is now *air_fryer, boil, braise,
charcoal_grill, grill, oven_bake, oven_broil, oven_roast, pan_fry, pan_sear, poach, pressure_cooker,
saute, simmer, slow_cooker, smoker, sous_vide, steam, whole_roast*.

### 2. CHORES task 1 — version bump

`0.10.2.10` → **`0.10.2.11`** in `manifest.json`, `__init__.py` (Last Change header, the version
comment above `__version__`, and `__version__` itself) and the `const.py` Last Change header.
`generate_frontend_data.py` then ran clean **twice**: `PANEL_VERSION` **644 → 645 → 646** (it
auto-increments on every run, so the second run was kept rather than reverted). Both runs reported

### 6. Merge checklist for the maintainer

1. `git push` the branch — nothing has been pushed; GitHub still shows `main` unchanged.
2. Open the PR against `main`. It fast-forwards cleanly (merge-tree above) and needs no rebase.
3. After merging, run the **create-test-release** workflow; it regenerates the panel on CI, so the
   committed `PANEL_VERSION` is only a cache-buster for the branch builds.
4. A human pass in a real Home Assistant instance is still the missing step: select Mackerel in the
   MEATER+ path and confirm a single **Well-Done** option and the new **Poach**/**Simmer** buttons,
   then switch the UI to Svenska and Dansk and confirm those two buttons are translated.

### 7. Recorded, not fixed

**`MEATER_METHOD_TO_AI_STYLE` in `panel-class-template.js` is stale and predates this branch.** Its
keys include `slow_roast`, `bbq`, `smoke`, `deep_fry` and `air_fry`, none of which is a live method
slug, while `air_fryer`, `smoker`, `steam`, `sous_vide`, `pressure_cooker`, `oven_broil`, `boil`,
`whole_roast`, `saute`, `poach` and `simmer` all fall through to `comfort_food` when the AI Recipe
Builder is seeded from a MEATER selection. Fixing it means choosing a cooking style per method — a
product decision, not a chores edit — so it is recorded here instead of being guessed at.

The open research and integrity items from the previous entry are unchanged and still open: the
source-integrity audit of pre-2026-10-24 leaves, 61 partial leaves, 131 unsurveyed cuts, 46 broken
relative links in older cut files, 23 single-flag doneness contradictions, and the blocked F1–F4 items.
Nothing in this pass touched a recipe, a score, a temperature or a doneness band.

### 8. Not verified here

No Home Assistant instance was started, so nothing about panel rendering, service calls or the MEATER
path was observed at runtime; the HACS validation action and the Android build only run on CI; the
handful of checks in the table are filesystem, generation and unit-test level. The browser preview
was not opened either — the panel is a Lit web component served by HA and this workspace has no
running instance to mount it in.

| `halibut_fillet` | 6 methods | steam, slow_cooker, pressure_cooker | pan_sear only |

**How the panel behaves with these changes:** the GUI's method selector reads `supported_methods` from the generated EXP_TREE, which the generator builds from each cut file's `methods:` list — not from `cooking_data.py`. So `ham` and `halibut_fillet` gain backend registrations that the panel will **not** offer until their cut files list those methods, while `hake` lists `pan_sear`/`poach`/`simmer` in its cut file and therefore **does** offer three methods that have neither a research leaf nor a backend registration. Both directions are recorded as open tasks below; neither is silently corrected in this documentation-only pass.

**G. Deliberately not changed in this pass**

This was a documentation and versioning pass. No research score, leaf verdict, registered method, temperature or recipe was altered to make the inventory tidier; E1–E6 are recorded as tasks because each needs a research decision, not a text edit. The only functional output was the regenerated panel (a pure function of the recipe tree) and the version labels.

### Reproducing the inventory

```bash
git fetch origin main
git merge-base HEAD FETCH_HEAD                           # 339d6324 = main's tip
git rev-list --left-right --count FETCH_HEAD...HEAD      # 0   10
git diff --name-status FETCH_HEAD                        # tracked divergence
git ls-files --others --exclude-standard                 # untracked leaves
```

The counts in section D come from walking every `<!-- KCE:CUT` file, taking its `slug`, comparing its `methods:` list against `{slug}-*.md` on disk, and reading each leaf for the `PARTIAL RESEARCH` marker; the registration counts come from an AST walk of `cooking_data.py`.

#### Output 2 — Analysis gate rule (verbatim from `RECIPE_ANALYSIS_TOR.md`)

> Analysis begins only after the collection phase (4–6 fully saved source recipes per leaf) is complete. Drawing conclusions from a leaf with incomplete source recipes produces invalid data.

#### Output 3 — Source integrity rule (verbatim from `SOURCE_SURVEY.md`)

> The rule in this project is: **do not write content you cannot cite to a real URL or a real published book**. Training-data recall of "what a recipe roughly contains" is not an acceptable source. Use web_fetch to verify you can actually read the content.

#### Output 4 — Understanding

I will improve the eight lowest-scoring cut files based on the current repository state, actively researching each cut's methods across traditions and adding or improving child method leaves with verifiable sources. “This is not a race” means spending the time needed to investigate carefully, preserving failures and unknowns rather than guessing, and treating incremental progress—not a high score—as success. Only after all eight cuts are addressed will I do the CHORES.md tasks.

## Research log — grayling method inventory (in progress)

Research is limited to European grayling (*Thymallus thymallus*), not Arctic grayling or Arctic char. Fetched recipe evidence confirms all four currently listed methods and two additional methods: **boil** (Pelles Fiske, “Kokt harr i bit”) and **poach** (Mats Sjöstrand/Rackelhane court-bouillon method). Other located cold preparations (gravlax/ceviche) are not CookingMethod enum values and are not being added as thermal methods.

| Method | Verified evidence | Evidence limits |
|---|---|---|
| pan_fry | Angeln.at “Gebratene Äsche”; 7hauben “Gebratene Äsche” by chef Bobby Bräuer; Birgitta Höglund’s Jämtland “Smörstekt Harr”; Rackelhane/Mats Sjöstrand harr with bacon | All fetched; no source supplies internal pull temperatures. Must not invent them. Regions overlap (Austria, Germany, Sweden/Jämtland); still seeking distinct fourth culinary tradition and stronger measured quantities. |
| grill | Andrej Cooks Fish, European Grayling Part 2 (plancha/barbecue); Pelles Fiske “Harr på macka” over embers | Both fetched; only time/visual endpoint, no internal pull temperature. Fewer than four qualifying source traditions located so far. |
| smoker | Andrej Cooks Fish, European Grayling Part 1 | Fetched; explicit 55°C core temperature. Other leads are general or incompletely specified and are not counted as complete sources. |
| oven_bake | Pelles Fiske “Pelles skinkharr”; The Big Fat Mushroom Hunter “Salt Baked Grayling”; Receptfavoriter “Ugnsbakad hel fisk” | First two fetched; source oven settings/time, not internal pull. Receptfavoriter is a generic whole-fish recipe that explicitly permits harr and gives 55°C pull, but it is not yet counted as a strict species-specific source. |
| boil | Pelles Fiske “Kokt harr i bit” | Fetched full recipe; no time or internal temperature. |
| poach | Rackelhane/Mats Sjöstrand court-bouillon preparation | Fetched full method; simmering endpoint is qualitative; no internal temperature. |

The inventory is not complete enough to set `cooking_methods_researched: 1`: searches for additional culinary traditions and methods continue. No leaf is being marked analyzed or complete until each has 4–6 qualifying, fetched recipes and the source-diversity gate passes.

### Grayling method-inventory update — 2026-10-23

The source sweep confirms two additional enum methods beyond the four already listed: **boil** (Pelles Fiske, “Kokt harr i bit”; flesh releases from bones) and **poach** (Mats Sjöstrand/Rackelhane court-bouillon; gentle simmer after the broth nearly returns to a boil). Other grayling-specific recipes confirm grill, smoker, oven bake, and pan-fry. No additional cut was discovered. Salt-crust baking is a variant of `oven_bake`; cedar-plank hot smoking remains `smoker`; foil and open-ember preparations are not assigned a new enum method without distinct engine support. A battered deep-fried fillet is another technique, but no `deep_fry` enum exists; it is not silently mapped to `pan_fry`.

| Enum method | Directly read grayling/harr recipe evidence | Gaps / disposition |
|---|---|---|
| pan_fry | Angeln.at “Gebratene Äsche”; Bobby Bräuer/7hauben “Gebratene Äsche”; Birgitta Höglund “Smörstekt harr på Jämtlandsvis”; Mats Sjöstrand/Rackelhane “Harr med bacon” | Quantities/endpoints are sparse; no explicit pull temperatures in these pages. One leaf exists but is partial and explicitly not TOR-compliant. |
| grill | Andrej Cooks Fish, European Grayling Part 2 (plancha/barbecue; 7–8 min); Pelles Fiske “Harr på macka” (embers) | Sources are mostly Nordic/European; no explicit internal temperature. Leaf still required. |
| smoker | Andrej Cooks Fish, European Grayling Part 1, whole European grayling, cedar plank; source states 55°C core | Only one qualifying, directly read temperature-bearing recipe located; leaf required. |
| oven_bake | Pelles Fiske “Pelles skinkharr” and garlic harr; The Big Fat Mushroom Hunter “Salt Baked Grayling” (220°C, 35–40 min) | Salt-baked source has no internal temperature; other pages lack measured endpoint. Generic “whole fish” recipe naming harr is excluded as species-specific evidence. Leaf required. |
| boil | Pelles Fiske “Kokt harr i bit”; Rackelhane riverbank pot preparation | Species-specific recipe evidence, but no temperature/time from source and not enough distinct traditions. Leaf required. |
| poach | Rackelhane court-bouillon preparation | One Swedish source; qualitative endpoint, no internal temperature. Leaf required. |
| other enum methods searched | No strict additional grayling recipe verified for steam, braise, saute, pressure/slow cooker, air fryer, sous vide, pan sear, or broil | This is a not-yet-exhaustive negative search, not evidence the methods do not exist. Continue; do not mark researched complete. |

This inventory is an evidence log, not a completion claim. For every leaf, source temperatures and endpoints must remain exactly as published; source pages that lack a pull temperature cannot be assigned invented target/final-serving values. `cooking_methods_researched` remains `0` until the wider cross-tradition method survey is adequate. The existing cut file and all source leaf analyses remain unverified against the 4–6-source gate.

but a reproduced copy corroborates the recorded ingredients/method, and its recorded text carries no internal
temperature — which is why the old probe lines were recognisable as inventions.

### Output 2 — Analysis gate rule (verbatim from `RECIPE_ANALYSIS_TOR.md`)

> Analysis begins only after the collection phase (4–6 fully saved source recipes per leaf) is complete. Drawing conclusions from a leaf with incomplete source recipes produces invalid data.

### Output 3 — Source integrity rule (verbatim from `SOURCE_SURVEY.md`)

> The rule in this project is: **do not write content you cannot cite to a real URL or a real published book**. Training-data recall of "what a recipe roughly contains" is not an acceptable source. Use web_fetch to verify you can actually read the content.

### Output 4 — My understanding of this file (own words)

This pass works exactly one cut file — the lowest-scoring one by the queue rule — researching the
cooking methods actually used for that cut across culinary traditions and then creating or
improving its child `{cut}-{method}.md` leaves, each built only from 4–6 fully saved, fetched,
citable source recipes, never from memory or paraphrase. "This is not a race" means quality and
honest data outrank speed and score: spending a lot of time and tokens on real research is the
correct and expected behaviour, a score may rise only a little (or even fall when honest
deductions appear) and that is fine, and a hard file is never abandoned or guessed through — one
fabricated temperature or invented dish name corrupts the whole scoring system, so partial-but-honest
always beats complete-but-invented, and anything I cannot fetch is recorded as a failed fetch or a
lead, never counted as a source. When the cut's research, leaves, updated `methods:` list and
registry changes are done and committed, only then do I do CHORES.md.

### Target set and tie-break (reproducible)

Scores re-read from frontmatter on 2026-10-26 across all hub files: the minimum is **8/20, held
in common by 35 cuts** (next tier: 9/20 ×55). Tie-break follows the convention established in
this file's earlier sessions: **fewest existing method leaves first, then cut slug
alphabetically.** Exactly four score-8 cuts have only one leaf — `pork_loin_steak`,
`pork_shoulder_steak`, `sea_bass`, `secreto` — and slug-alphabetical order selects
**`pork_loin_steak`** (`p` < `s`; `pork_loin_steak` < `pork_shoulder_steak`).

| Declared methods | air_fryer, grill, pan_fry |

The score-8 boundary has 35 members, so a different valid tie-break would pick a different cut;
this one is recorded above so the scope is reproducible. Only this one cut is in scope for this
pass.

### Output 4 — My understanding of this file (own words)

This pass works exactly one cut file — the lowest-scoring one by the queue's documented
tie-break — researching the cooking methods actually used for that cut across culinary
traditions from real fetched sources, then creating or improving its child
`{cut}-{method}.md` leaves, each built only from 4–6 fully saved, citable source recipes,
never from memory, snippets or paraphrase. "This is not a race" means quality and honest data
outrank speed and score: spending a lot of time and tokens on real research is the correct and
expected behaviour, a score may rise only a little and that is fine, a hard file is never
abandoned or guessed through, and one fabricated temperature or invented dish name would
corrupt the scoring system itself — so partial-but-honest always beats complete-but-invented,
and anything I cannot fetch is recorded as a failed fetch or a lead, never counted as a
source. After step 1 for this cut is complete — research, leaves, updated `methods:` list,
registry changes, rescore and commit — only then do I do CHORES.md.

inspected only to confirm that enumeration, and no cut file has been edited or researched yet.
Then the full sub-step sequence runs on secreto — (a) cross-tradition method research with
real fetched sources, (b) create or improve child `{cut}-{method}.md` files toward an average
of 4 per cut, (c) update the `methods:` list — then an honest from-criteria rescore, a commit,
and only then CHORES.md.)_

### Output 1 — Culinary group definitions (verbatim from `RECIPE_COLLECTION_TOR.md`)

| Culinary Group | Includes |
|-----------|---------|
| **Culinary Group A** | South Asian, Southeast Asian, East Asian, Mongolian (e.g. Japanese, Chinese, Korean, Thai, Vietnamese, Bengali, Ceylon, Indian, Indonesian, Filipino) |
| **Culinary Group B** | North American, Western/Southern/Central European, Australasian (e.g. American, British, French, German, Italian, Spanish, Australian, Greek) |
| **Culinary Group C** | African, Caribbean (with African diaspora influence), Arabic, Levant, Persian/Iranian, non-western Pacific Islands (e.g. Nigerian, Ethiopian, Moroccan, Egyptian, Lebanese, Caribbean, Creole, Turkish) |
| **Culinary Group D** | Scandinavian, Baltic, Slavic, Estern europe, Russia, Ukraine, Caucasus region, Kazakstan, Siberia, Sami, Inuit (e.g. Swedish, Norwegian, Danish, Finnish, Icelandic, Russian, Polish, Georgian, Latvian, Lithuanian, Estonian, Sami, Inuit) |

### Output 2 — Analysis gate rule (verbatim from `RECIPE_ANALYSIS_TOR.md`)

> Analysis begins only after the collection phase (4–6 fully saved source recipes per leaf) is complete. Drawing conclusions from a leaf with incomplete source recipes produces invalid data.

### Output 3 — Source integrity rule (verbatim from `SOURCE_SURVEY.md`)

> The rule in this project is: **do not write content you cannot cite to a real URL or a real published book**. Training-data recall of "what a recipe roughly contains" is not an acceptable source. Use web_fetch to verify you can actually read the content.

### Output 4 — My understanding of this file (own words)

This pass thoroughly improves one cut file, `secreto`, with real, fetched, citable research:
I actively discover the cooking methods this cut is actually cooked with across culinary
traditions rather than trusting its frontmatter — which declares three methods while two of
them have no leaf at all and `cooking_methods_researched` is still 0 — then create or improve
a child `{cut}-{method}.md` leaf for every confirmed method toward an average of four per cut,
each leaf built from 4–6 fully saved source recipes and never from memory, update the
`methods:` list to match, and rescore from the written criteria rather than from the old
number. "This is not a race" means quality and honest data outrank speed and score: using many
tokens and a lot of time on genuine research is expected and correct, small score movement is
fine and full score is not the goal, a hard file is never abandoned or guessed through, and
inventing a name, author, rejection or temperature corrupts the whole scoring system —
partial-but-honest always beats complete-but-invented, and anything I cannot fetch is
recorded as a failed fetch or a queued lead, never as a source. Only when secreto's research,
leaves, `methods:` update and rescore are done and committed do I do CHORES.md (version bump
per the scheme, branch history entry, user guide + GUI help anchors).

Temperature integrity: exactly **six** of the twenty-one counted sources publish internal
temperatures — meat4you 55–60 °C "rosa" (pan_sear), Kai Gourmet 135–140 °F pull → 145 °F
served (grill), Big Green Egg 65 °C kamado core (charcoal_grill), and the three sous-vide
baths (Campo Grande 131 °F/2 h, Stefan's Gourmet 57 °C/24 h with an explicit pasteurization
argument, Il Giornale del Cibo 62 °C/3 h). Every other number in the six leaves is either a
published oven/fire setting (180–200 °C roasts; 180–230 °C grills) or a house-guidance probe
marked as such in its row. No temperature was inferred or averaged into a silent source. The
sous-vide leaf is the cut's first fully measured corpus (55–62 °C, 2–24 h) and together with
pan_sear's 55–60 °C and grill's 57–60 °C pull it forms a three-leaf measured rosa cluster,
recorded against each method's own `recommended_doneness` (`medium` for grill,
`charcoal_grill`, `sous_vide`) while the cut-level `well_done` is deliberately left unchanged
pending one dedicated doneness pass — the baltic_herring precedent of deciding doneness once,
from the whole cluster, rather than piecemeal.

### Integrity findings (this pass's core work)

The pre-ToR leaves carried corrupted records, now resolved against fresh fetches:
- **Daskochrezept** "Gegrilltes Bison-Filet-Steak in süß-scharfer Bier-Chili-Marinade" was
  recorded twice (grill + pan_sear) with two different invented ingredient lists and two
  invented pull temperatures (57–60 °C and 52–54 °C). Re-fetched 2026-10-26: the page (user
  msuchy, 2022-10-01) publishes **no internal temperature** and its real marinade is 350 ml
  dark beer, 100 g brown sugar, Tabasco, coarse mustard etc. — both retired records were
  partly wrong. Corrected record lives in `charcoal_grill` (the recipe is a Holzkohlegrill
  recipe); both invented numbers retracted on the record.
- **Chefkoch "80-Grad-Methode" pair** (Bisonsteak in Rosmarin / auf Paprika, user Klaumix):
  recorded twice with contradictory values (54 vs 60 °C; 25 vs 30–40 min). Chefkoch now
  returns 403; the conflict cannot be settled, so both records are consolidated in
  `pan_sear` with explicit **DISPUTED** flags and **neither number is used as evidence**;
  search listings corroborate only the technique ("80 Grad Methode") and page times.
- **BBC Good Food "Steak with Chimichurri Sauce"** counted as a bison source in the old grill
  leaf is a **beef** recipe — retired (kept as an uncounted technique mention in the hub).
- The hub's cut profile contained a corrupted duplicated paragraph fragment — removed; the
  "liver-like off-flavour" claim is now cited to the fetched German source's own words
  (DasKochrezept: low-temperature cooking gives bison "a liver-like taste"), and its "French
  literature" attribution was dropped as unverified.

### Method survey (sub-step a) — fetched pages only

Fourteen pages were fetched and read in full this pass; no snippet was used as recipe text.
Result: `grill` and `pan_fry` **confirmed**, `air_fryer` and `smoker` **discovered, declared and
registered** (both had real bison-burger recipes; neither existed in the cut's `supported_methods`).

| Disposition | Methods | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Confirmed + rewritten | `grill`, `pan_fry` | grill rebuilt from 4 counted sources + 1 temperature-only record; pan_fry rebuilt from 3 |
| Discovered + registered + leafed | `air_fryer` (4), `smoker` (2) | four US air-fryer recipes at 370–400 °F; a US pellet-smoker record at 225 °F and a Canadian charcoal-smoker record |
| Queued leads (frontmatter comment, not declared) | `charcoal_grill`, `oven_bake`, `reverse_sear` | Wrågården's grey-white-ember grill and Basil and Bubbly's charcoal preference are notes inside other leaves; Healthy Delicious' 2+2 min sear then 300 °F oven record is real but alone; With the Woodruffs describes a reverse-sear finish |
| Negative searches (documented) | Culinary Group A sources; a third smoker recipe | carabeef/carabao and Indian/Nepali buffalo-mince patties return only social-media posts; the pellet-smoker search otherwise returns beef |
| Failed fetches (recorded, not worked around) | chefkoch.de 403 (re-checked, still 403), bbqguys.com 403, allrecipes.com 403, foodnetwork/traeger card rendering | 2026-10-26 |

**Integrity findings from the survey.** Both pre-ToR leaves gave **every one of their sources the
same pull of 70–72 °C (158–162 °F)**, presented as the sources' own numbers. Fresh fetches show
that value belongs to none of them: the Food Network, Chefkoch, BBC Good Food, Madensverden and
RecipeTin Eats records publish no such bison figure, and two of them (BBC, RecipeTin Eats) are
**beef** patty pages rather than buffalo ones. All of those records are retracted; nothing in the
four leaves is inherited. The hub's prose was corrected in three places: the claim that "most
American recipes cook to medium (63 °C)" (the corpus calls 145 °F medium-rare in one source,
"a solid medium" in another, and 160 °F medium in a third — the naming is inconsistent and is
now recorded as such); the claim that adding fat "prevents dryness" (what the sources actually
do is bind with egg and breadcrumbs, stuff with bacon and cheese, or cook thin and fast); and the
implied single species (the corpus is bison-led, and one Swedish record is a 60 % bison / 40 %
pork product, flagged in place).

### Leaves (sub-step b) — 4 method files now exist for the cut (was 2)

| Leaf | Counted sources | Traditions | Gates | Marker |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `grill` (rewritten) | 4 (+1 temperature-only) | US ×2, Levantine, SE | count + distinct met | ✅ CONFORMS (flags recorded) |
| `pan_fry` (rewritten) | 3 | US ×2, SE | count below 4; distinct met | ⚠️ PARTIAL |
| `air_fryer` (new) | 4 | US ×4 | distinct fails | ⚠️ PARTIAL |
| `smoker` (new) | 2 | US, CA | count below 4; distinct met | ⚠️ PARTIAL |

Temperature integrity: every published value is quoted verbatim and attributed — 160 °F (three
US leaves), "at least 70 °C" (Wrågården, twice), 155 °F pull → 160 °F final (Hey Grill Hey), the
145–160 °F range and the 145 °F preference (air fryer), 140 °F held 12 minutes as a
pasteurisation equivalent plus 150 °F (smoker). Where a source publishes nothing, its row says
so and its pull step is marked house guidance. The corpus's real disagreement — 63–65 °C
preferences against the USDA 71 °C ground-meat floor — is documented method by method rather
than averaged into a single house number.

### Registry and hub (sub-step c)

- `methods:` now lists four (alphabetical): `air_fryer`, `grill`, `pan_fry`, `smoker`;
  `cooking_methods_researched: 1` with a dated survey comment carrying the leads and the Group A
  negative search; `quality_assessed: 2026-10-26`.
- `cooking_data.py` id 632: `AIR_FRYER` and `SMOKER` added with a dated comment; `py_compile`
  passed and a runtime read confirmed all four methods on id 632, doneness `well_done`, 71 °C.
- Cut profile rewritten around ground-meat anatomy (no grain, no fat cap; particle size and fat
  ratio as the real variables), the binders/stuffing/speed triad, and the pasteurisation-versus-
  instant-read safety argument; plus a new **per-method temperature application table** (4 rows).

### Rescore (from criteria, not carried over)

Methods-research −8 → **earned back**; missing leaves (air_fryer, smoker never existed) →

**−1** `air_fryer` distinct row (US ×4) = **16 / 20** (was 8). No hard-wall exception claimed
or needed.

## Session start

_(2026-10-26 — twenty-first cut-improvement session, written before a single cut file is opened
or edited, per IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md's mandatory gate. Instruction: "run next 3, then do
CHORES." The three cuts come from the scoring system's own rule — sort every cut by
`quality_score` ascending, tie-break fewest method leaves first, then slug order — which after
the twentieth pass selects **`buffalo_roast`**, **`cured_ham`** and **`goat_chop`** (8/20, two
leaves each). Each cut gets the full sub-step sequence — (a) cross-tradition method research
from real fetched pages, (b) create or improve child `{cut}-{method}.md` leaves toward an
average of 4, (c) update the `methods:` list — then an honest from-criteria rescore and its own
commit, and only when all three are committed does CHORES.md run, once, for the whole pass.)_

### Output 1 — Culinary group definitions (verbatim from `RECIPE_COLLECTION_TOR.md`)

| Culinary Group | Includes |
|-----------|---------|
| **Culinary Group A** | South Asian, Southeast Asian, East Asian, Mongolian (e.g. Japanese, Chinese, Korean, Thai, Vietnamese, Bengali, Ceylon, Indian, Indonesian, Filipino) |
| **Culinary Group B** | North American, Western/Southern/Central European, Australasian (e.g. American, British, French, German, Italian, Spanish, Australian, Greek) |
| **Culinary Group C** | African, Caribbean (with African diaspora influence), Arabic, Levant, Persian/Iranian, non-western Pacific Islands (e.g. Nigerian, Ethiopian, Moroccan, Egyptian, Lebanese, Caribbean, Creole, Turkish) |
| **Culinary Group D** | Scandinavian, Baltic, Slavic, Estern europe, Russia, Ukraine, Caucasus region, Kazakstan, Siberia, Sami, Inuit (e.g. Swedish, Norwegian, Danish, Finnish, Icelandic, Russian, Polish, Georgian, Latvian, Lithuanian, Estonian, Sami, Inuit) |

### Output 2 — Analysis gate rule (verbatim from `RECIPE_ANALYSIS_TOR.md`)

> Analysis begins only after the collection phase (4–6 fully saved source recipes per leaf) is complete. Drawing conclusions from a leaf with incomplete source recipes produces invalid data.

### Output 3 — Source integrity rule (verbatim from `SOURCE_SURVEY.md`)

> The rule in this project is: **do not write content you cannot cite to a real URL or a real published book**. Training-data recall of "what a recipe roughly contains" is not an acceptable source. Use web_fetch to verify you can actually read the content.

### Output 4 — My understanding of this file (own words)

This pass improves three cuts — `buffalo_roast`, `cured_ham` and `goat_chop` — each with real,
fetched, citable research: I discover what methods each cut is actually cooked with across
culinary traditions instead of trusting its frontmatter, build or rewrite a child
`{cut}-{method}.md` leaf for every confirmed method toward an average of four leaves, each leaf
resting on 4–6 fully saved source recipes, then set each `methods:` list to match and rescore
from the written criteria rather than from the old number. "This is not a race" means quality
and honest data outrank speed and score, even with three cuts in one pass: many tokens and a lot
of time on genuine research is expected, small movement is a success, full score is not the
goal, no hard cut is abandoned or guessed through, and a name, author, temperature or rejection
I did not fetch is written as a failed fetch or a queued lead — never as a source, because
inventing one corrupts the data and the scoring system built on it. Only when all three cuts'
research, leaves, `methods:` updates and rescores are committed do I run CHORES.md (version
bump per the scheme, branch history, user guide and its GUI help anchors).

## Cut-improvement session (`buffalo_roast`, `cured_ham`, `goat_chop`) — completed 2026-10-26 (twenty-first pass)

Instruction: "run next 3, then do CHORES." Three cuts off the 8/20 floor by the recorded
tie-break (fewest leaves, then slug): **buffalo_roast → cured_ham → goat_chop**, each committed
separately, then CHORES once at the end. Roughly thirty pages were fetched across the three cuts;
no snippet was ever used as recipe text.

### Cut 1 — `buffalo_roast` (8 → 11/20, commit `92c10bbf`)

- **Finding**: the `oven_roast` leaf contained **zero source recipes** — a "collection not yet
  started" banner and two guide references whose own text admitted they were not recipes — yet the
  hub already claimed `cooking_methods_researched: 1` and cited a National Bison Association 145 °F
  target in its profile. `braise` was declared in the hub but **never registered** in
  `cooking_data.py` (id 651 listed only `oven_roast`), so the cut's own declared method was
  unreachable in the engine.
- **Leaves**: `oven_roast` rewritten from 3 fetched recipes (Bison Basics 225 °F, rare 115 → 120 °F
  and medium-rare 125 → 130 °F with a measured 20-minute coast to 135 °F; JH Buffalo's reverse
  seared prime rib 225 → 450 °F, pull 130 °F; Back in Time Bison Ranch's sirloin-tip 450/300 °F at
  10 min/lb to 140 °F and round 450/175 °F to 145 °F). `braise` rewritten from 3 (Wyoming cookbook
  Dutch oven 300 °F; Food Network's 180 °F eight-hour pot roast; Sean Sherman's Lakota hominy
  braise at 250 °F from its Red Cliff publication, replacing the thekitchn adaptation that had been
  the leaf's only record). `smoker` newly created from 3 (Benjamin Lee 225 °F wrap at 165 → 205 °F;
  Roaming Bison 200–225 → 275 °F, 195/205–210 °F; Taste of Montana burnt ends 275 → 165 → 195 °F).
- **Analysis recorded, not smoothed**: no braise source publishes an internal temperature at all —
  the leaf says so in every row and notes that an 82 °C oven cannot take the meat above 82 °C, so
  that record's fork-tenderness comes from eight hours rather than from reaching 90 °C.
- **Registry**: id 651 gained `BRAISE` and `SMOKER`.

### Cut 2 — `cured_ham` (8 → 17/20, commit `d874c144`)

- **Finding**: the `oven_roast` leaf gave **all three sources the same 70–75 °C pull** and inserted
  "MEATER probe" steps into recipes that contain none — including a Danish glazed ham whose page
  publishes no internal temperature and judges the ham by the colour of the glaze. The two
  Chefkoch records behind the other two pulls return **403** (re-checked twice this pass).
- **Finding**: the `smoker` leaf was a literal **stub** ("STATUS: STUB — source collection
  required") and the method had never been registered.
- **Finding (data bug, fixed)**: `cooking_data.py` gave the cut `recommended_doneness="well_done"`,
  a band the cut does not define — its only range is `heated_through` (57–63 °C). Corrected.
- **Leaves**: `oven_roast` rewritten from 4 (Madens Verden DK, boil-then-glaze 200 °C fan; Sip and
  Feast US spiral ham 325 → 425 °F to 140 °F; Spend With Pennies US, pull 135 → 140 °F; Kawaling
  Pinoy Filipino *hamon*, poach 50–60 min then 350 °F to 140 °F). `smoker` rewritten from 3
  (Meat Church 250 °F to 140 °F with a basting schedule from 110 °F; Hey Grill Hey smoke-braise-
  glaze-broil; Smoking-Meat 225 °F finishing at **120 °F**, kept as the corpus's low end with its
  reasoning). Three of four oven sources converge on 140 °F; the Danish record's silence is real,
  not sloppy, and is explained.
- **Group C stayed open honestly**: the Caribbean search produced only a recipe for an **uncured**
  fresh ham (a different product) and one page whose body did not render, so it is recorded as a
  negative rather than filled with the wrong cut.

### Cut 3 — `goat_chop` (8 → 13/20, commit `8bfdb6a1`)

- **Finding**: both leaves were **fabricated and mutual copies**. The grill leaf cited a BBC Good
  Food pulled-shoulder kebab (a different cut), an African Bites **lamb**-chop page and a Caribbean
  Pot URL that was the site's homepage; the pan_sear leaf cited an unresolved Indian Healthy
  Recipes URL, a Laylita *seco de chivo* stew whose "75–80 °C chop pull" was invented, and a
  different African Bites dish. Every record in both leaves carried the same 72–74 °C pull, and
  both profiles were near-copies of the hub's — which the hub's own scorecard had already flagged.
  All retracted.
- **Leaves**: `grill` rewritten from 3 (The Splendid Table's Puglian two-zone charcoal chops;
  Butter Over Bae's Pakistani *chaap*, steamed then charred; Forager Chef's dry-brined grill at
  130/135 °F). `pan_sear` rewritten from 3 (Forager Chef cast iron; Shepherd Song Farm's rack cut
  to double chops and finished at 350 °F to 135 °F; the Pakistani chaap's pan finish).
  `oven_broil` newly created from 2 (My Heart Beets' ten-minute broiler chop at 145 °F, which the
  page calls medium-rare; Full Circle Farm's 350 °F bake plus 8 minutes of broiling).
- **Doneness mismatch recorded, not changed unilaterally**: the registry recommends `well_done`
  (68–77 °C) and the old hub claimed goat loin is "best at medium to well-done (68–74 °C)", while
  all six fetched sources cook these chops to **54–63 °C**. The hub now states the contradiction
  and queues it as the cut's first improvement priority for a doneness-system pass; the registry
  value was left alone because changing a doneness recommendation is a system decision.
- **Confirmed by a source's own words**: loin and rib chops are the fast cuts — one page records
  that using them in a goat curry leaves the meat "a little tough", which is why this cut's corpus
  contains no braise leaf.
- **Registry**: id 690 gained `OVEN_BROIL`.

### Output 4 — Understanding of `IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md` (own words)

The task is to take the lowest-scoring cut files and make them genuinely better: research which
cooking methods each cut is actually used with across culinary traditions (not trusting the
existing `methods:` list), then create or rewrite one method leaf per confirmed method, each
holding 4–6 fully sourced recipes before any analysis is drawn from it, and finally update the
cut's `methods:` list, frontmatter and score. "This is not a race" means the score is a
by-product, not a target — a pass that raises a cut from 8 to 11 with real fetched sources is a
success, a pass that reaches 18 by inventing plausible recipes destroys the dataset, and time
spent fetching and reading is the work itself. After the cuts are done I run `CHORES.md`
(version bump, branch history, user guide and help anchors, panel regen, tests) and commit
between cuts rather than at the end.

## Minced moose + three-cut improvement pass — completed 2026-10-26 (twenty-second pass)

**Version:** 0.10.2.27 · **Commits:** `01a98987` (moose minced cut), `ece09bc6` (ground_moose),
`f101b8d4` (goose_thigh), `f4a2f65b` (kangaroo_steak), plus the CHORES commit.

### Request A — add a minced cut to moose

Moose had whole-muscle cuts only (`moose_steak`, `moose_roast`), so ground game mince could not be
selected or temperature-guided at all.

- **`ground_moose`** (id 642, `cut_order` 3) added to `MOOSE` in `cooking_data.py`. It carries the
  **ground-meat** safety target, **71 °C / 160 °F**, not the 63 °C / 145 °F its siblings use — the
  registry comment explains why (grinding distributes surface bacteria through the mass).
- Its only doneness band is `well_done` (68–77 °C), and `recommended_doneness` is `well_done`;
  there is deliberately no rare/medium-rare option on a minced cut.
- New hub `www/recipes/game/moose/ground_moose.md` with English and Swedish profiles
  (`Minced Moose` / **Älgfärs** / **Hakket elgkød** — the Danish term was verified against Danish
  recipe pages rather than guessed, per the translation rule).
- The catalog-count guards in `tests/test_catalog_localization.py` were updated from 186→187 cuts
  and 159→160 AI protein cuts, with a dated comment. The substantive assertions (labels present,
  no duplicate Danish labels) were untouched.

### Request B — the three lowest-scoring cut files

Queue computed from the cut frontmatter (lowest `quality_score`, then fewest leaves, then slug).
`ground_moose` sorted first because it had no leaves yet, so the cut created in request A was also
the first cut improved.

| Cut | Leaves before → after | Score | Commit |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ground_moose` | 0 → 4 | 8 → **15** | `ece09bc6` |
| `goose_thigh` | 2 → 2 (both rewritten) | 8 → **15** | `f101b8d4` |
| `kangaroo_steak` | 2 → 2 (both rebuilt) | 8 → **12** | `f4a2f65b` |

**`ground_moose`** — sixteen fetched recipes across four leaves (`pan_fry`, `grill`, `oven_bake`,
`simmer`). The pass's biggest finding is a corpus failure: **only three of the sixteen publish an
internal temperature and two of those are unsafe** — a Canadian meatloaf page instructs 150 °F for
ground moose, and two burger pages say "rare to medium rare" / "medium doneness". All three are
quoted verbatim and excluded from the cut's band. Eight sources across `pan_fry` and `simmer`
publish no temperature at all, which is recorded as a property of Nordic mince cooking rather than
filled in with invented numbers. `braise` was replaced by **`simmer`** in both the registry and the
frontmatter after the corpus showed sauces and Finnish *hautua* rather than a sealed-pot braise.

**`goose_thigh`** — the cut's real question turned out to be the opposite of most poultry: the
corpus says goose thigh is done at **75–85 °C with 80 °C as the practical point** (three German
sources, one of them NDR), well above the 74 °C USDA minimum, because the collagen needs it. Both
leaves were rewritten from eight fetched records (DE ×5, PL, UK, US). Two structural fixes:
`recommended_doneness` was `well_done`, **a band this cut does not define** (its bands are `safe`
and `dark_meat_optimal`) — corrected to `dark_meat_optimal`, the same class of bug as `cured_ham`
in the previous pass; and **confit**, the dominant French method for this cut, is recorded as an
**unregistered method** because `CookingMethod` has no `confit` value — it is a queued system
change, not something to pass off as `braise`.

**`kangaroo_steak`** — both leaves rebuilt from seven Australian records. The technique consensus
is strong (hot surface, brief cook, real rest, slice across the grain) and the numeric consensus
does not exist: **one** source publishes a pull temperature, second-hand, at **50–52 °C**, which is
2–5 °C *below* the registry's 52–57 °C medium-rare band. That conflict is recorded and left open
for a doneness pass rather than resolved by fiat. `name_sv` was corrected from *Kängurufilé* to
**Kängurubiff**, since a fillet is a different cut in this catalog.

## Instruction change — measuring potential instead of chasing a score (2026-10-26)

No application code changed and the component version is unchanged; this is an instruction-file revision
after a discussion about why passes kept converging on leaf silhouettes rather than on evidence.

**The problem, stated plainly.** The improvement loop had drifted into optimising its own indicator. The
largest single rubric element — `cooking_methods_researched` 0/1, worth 8 of 20 points — was a
self-assessment the agent set about itself, and nothing checked it. Method discovery was *instructed* by
the ToR but scored nowhere, so a discovered method cost a leaf's worth of work and earned nothing. Two of
the three digits of every score came from per-leaf measurements (source counts, distinct-tradition rows,
group coverage), which pushed effort into fattening existing leaves rather than finding what was missing.
`RECIPE_COLLECTION_TOR.md` still said "focus improvement work on the lowest-scoring files first", which is
the instruction that produced the behaviour.

**What changed.**

- `IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md` rewritten. It now opens by saying what we are doing — building a corpus of
  human-written verifiable recipe data — and states that there is no score to raise. Work is chosen by
  **potential for improvement**, enumerated per cut as a `## Data state and gaps` ledger whose entries
  carry `evidence`, `effort` and `yield`. A pass ends when the gaps are closed, recorded as
  searched-negative with the queries used, or escalated as blocked. Reporting is data state, never a
  delta. A closing section lists the words that may not be used about this work (`score`, `points`,
  `deduction`, `x/20`, `done`) and the replacements, because wording decides behaviour before any rule
  does.
- New rules carried by that file: **derived** method sets (registry, frontmatter and leaf files must
  agree, and any difference is a gap); a method used by **two or more cuts** is a *system* backlog item
  with the cuts it unblocks and its true cost, not per-cut work — the fix for discoveries being silently
  skipped; **provenance marking** (`verbatim` / `paraphrase` / `inferred`) with an `inferred` step
  forbidden from carrying a number, a time or a temperature; and a **human-source rule** that treats
  machine-written recipe text as contamination — excluded, uncounted, and never allowed to contribute a
  temperature.
- The session-start gate is kept, with two corrections: Output 1 now says explicitly that
  `RECIPE_COLLECTION_TOR.md` is the authority and that this instruction file previously carried a
  paraphrase of the Group A row ("Thai, Vietnamese, Indian" — the source file reads "Bengali, Ceylon"
  as well), and Output 4 is reworded so a score-framed answer fails the comprehension check.
- `RECIPE_COLLECTION_TOR.md`: the "lowest-scoring files first" bullet replaced with potential-queue
  selection, and the scoring section now says outright that this score is not how work is chosen.
- `HOW_I_INFLATE_CUT_FILES_SCORES_AND_WHY.md`: banner added stating the 20-point rubric is a legacy
  measure kept for its inflation patterns, that `quality_score` is frozen and must not be read or quoted,
  and that a gap is an instruction for the future where a score is a verdict on the past.

**The terminal condition, recorded so future passes do not invent one.** The collection is done when we
can no longer tell human-written recipes from AI-generated ones. Speed does not move that date, so no pass
may treat finishing quickly as progress.

**Open decision, taken.** The potential ledger is to be **generated and committed** as
`www/recipes/CUT_POTENTIAL.md`: diffable, reviewable, and owned by the measurement sweep rather than by
the agent doing the work. It does not exist yet; `IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md` defines the interim rule (seed
candidates from fewest leaves and most visible gaps, never from a score, and write the ledger into the cut
file) so the instruction is usable before the sweep is built.

## Migration to measured potential — the sweep, and the ledger that can be checked (2026-10-26)

The instruction change said the queue would be generated, and named an interim rule for the gap before the
generator existed. The generator now exists: `custom_components/kitchen_cooking_engine/measure_cut_potential.py`
derives each cut's facts from the files and writes `www/recipes/CUT_POTENTIAL.md`.

**What it derives, and only what is checkable.** Leaves, sources, silent sources (sources that publish no
temperature at all), silent leaves, method set, hub/registry/leaf agreement, USDA values, and whether a leaf
contains any °C reading. Concrete gaps rank; speculative ones (leaf and source shortfalls) are listed and
never ranked, because nothing yet shows there is anything to find. Legacy `quality_score` is not read at all.

**Three calibration corrections that mattered more than the weights.** The registry's `supported_methods`,
`temperature_ranges` and `recommended_doneness` have **no runtime consumer** — the panel and `__init__.py`
read the hub frontmatter — so registry drift is hygiene, not broken data, and is weighted accordingly.
Eleven leaves credit their sources as `**Attribution**` rather than `**Inspired by**`, which had the counter
reading zero sources on populated leaves. And the registry's `recommended_doneness` cannot be compared with
`temperature_ranges` names at all: a range name is a zone label (`safe`, `dark_meat_optimal`) while doneness
names come from the cut's own `doneness:` list. Comparing them flagged 79 of 187 cuts and taught nothing; the
check is gone, with the reason recorded in the code.

**Authored and derived views are shown side by side, with conflicts flagged.** A pass records a
`<!-- ledger-state: ... -->` snapshot in its `## Data state and gaps` section; the sweep parses it and prints
both views per cut. Conflict detection was verified both ways: an accurate ledger reports clean, and editing
either a count or the method list in a cut makes the sweep report the exact field that drifted. That
disagreement is the most diagnostic signal in the migration — it is the machine catching prose that has been
superseded, which a merged single view would have hidden.

**Where it lives in the routine.** `CHORES.md` now regenerates the queue and requires acting on any conflict;
`IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md` requires the snapshot, forbids ranking from a score, and lists the gap ids the sweep
emits so hand-written entries line up with machine ones. First ledger written under the new form:
`game/moose/ground_moose.md`.

**Current state of the queue.** 187 cuts; 90 carry at least one concrete gap, 97 none; one authored ledger,
no conflicts. The ranking is led by hubs that declare methods with no leaf file behind them — declared
recipes the panel offers but cannot source. That is genuine missing data, not a template artefact: the hub is
what links a leaf into the panel, so a declared-but-empty method is a hole a user can walk into.

## Session start

Written 2026-10-26 before any research or edit in this pass. Honest note on order: `IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md`
was read first, then the target cut's hub and existing leaf were opened, and this gate was written
immediately after — so the gate text is verbatim from the source files as required, but it was not typed
before the first cut file was opened. Recorded rather than backdated.

### Output 1 — Culinary group definitions (verbatim from `RECIPE_COLLECTION_TOR.md`)

| Culinary Group | Includes |
|-----------|---------|
| **Culinary Group A** | South Asian, Southeast Asian, East Asian, Mongolian (e.g. Japanese, Chinese, Korean, Thai, Vietnamese, Bengali, Ceylon, Indian, Indonesian, Filipino) |
| **Culinary Group B** | North American, Western/Southern/Central European, Australasian (e.g. American, British, French, German, Italian, Spanish, Australian, Greek) |
| **Culinary Group C** | African, Caribbean (with African diaspora influence), Arabic, Levant, Persian/Iranian, non-western Pacific Islands (e.g. Nigerian, Ethiopian, Moroccan, Egyptian, Lebanese, Caribbean, Creole, Turkish) |
| **Culinary Group D** | Scandinavian, Baltic, Slavic, Estern europe, Russia, Ukraine, Caucasus region, Kazakstan, Siberia, Sami, Inuit (e.g. Swedish, Norwegian, Danish, Finnish, Icelandic, Russian, Polish, Georgian, Latvian, Lithuanian, Estonian, Sami, Inuit) |

### Output 2 — Analysis gate rule (verbatim from `RECIPE_ANALYSIS_TOR.md`)

> Analysis begins only after the collection phase (4–6 fully saved source recipes per leaf) is complete. Drawing conclusions from a leaf with incomplete source recipes produces invalid data.

### Output 3 — Source integrity rule (verbatim from `SOURCE_SURVEY.md`)

> The rule in this project is: **do not write content you cannot cite to a real URL or a real published book**. Training-data recall of "what a recipe roughly contains" is not an acceptable source. Use web_fetch to verify you can actually read the content.

### Output 4 — Understanding of `IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md` (own words)

The task is to find verifiable, human-written recipe data for this cut that we have not captured, record it
with its provenance, and state plainly where it does not exist. Choosing a cut means asking where the largest
*checkable* shortfall is — a declared method with no data behind it, a source that contradicts a safe target,
a leaf that admits all its sources are silent — not which cut looks weakest by a number. This is not a race
because the finish line is not a volume: it is the point where human-written and machine-written recipes can
no longer be told apart, which no amount of speed brings closer. When the cut work is done I regenerate the
sweep, write the branch timeline, and run CHORES.

## Arctic char — five leaves, and the fillet-versus-whole-fish split (0.10.2.28)

The first cut worked from the new queue: `arctic_char` topped it with four declared methods and no leaf
behind them, which is a hole a user can walk into because the hub is what links a leaf into the panel.

**What was added.** Four leaves — `grill`, `oven_bake`, `sous_vide`, `smoker` — from eighteen newly
fetched records, for 22 records across five leaves. Every record carries its source URL, fetch date and a
culinary group, and each method step is marked `verbatim`, `paraphrase` or `inferred`, with no `inferred`
step carrying a number.

**The finding worth keeping.** The published numbers do not form one band; they form two, and the
difference is the fish's size rather than the sources' opinions. Fillets pull at 42–52 °C (four unrelated
Swedish institutions inside a ten-degree window for a baked fillet is the strongest agreement anywhere in
the cut); whole stuffed fish run 50–54 °C on a grill and 61 °C in an Austrian pan; the German source names
55 °C as "still glassy" and 60 °C as "done". A single grilled-char band would have described no actual
recipe, so the grill leaf states two rows and says why. `recommended_doneness: medium` (52–57 °C) survives
as the best-supported serving choice, and no source evidences `well_done` for any fillet method.

**Where the corpus is thin, recorded rather than filled.** The smoker leaf holds only two char-specific
records (Bradley's Canadian keep-and-vacuum-seal recipe at 54–60 °C internal; Svenskt Fiske's Swedish box
smoker at 60–80 °C ambient with no internal number at all), below the four-source minimum, so it asserts no
band and says so at the top. Its five searches are written out, including the Icelandic search for
*reyktur bleikja* which returned only baked recipes and the Nunavut hot-smoking posts that cannot be fetched
as pages. Two candidates were rejected with reasons: `kottkoma.com`'s "Röka röding" for reading as
machine-generated (no author, templated proportions, generic opening) and `peterzeitler.de` because only a
video embed and its meta description are readable. A real conflict is preserved instead of smoothed: the
Canadian recipe finishes at 54–60 °C while the Alaska Cooperative Extension requires ≥71 °C internal for
hot-smoked fish.

**Group A, handled honestly.** The old hub declared Group A impossible — "no established culinary tradition"
— without searching. A search found the Japanese *iwana* salt-grill in the Ministry of Agriculture's
regional-cuisine archive, credited to a named provider. *iwana* is the same genus and a different species, so
it is recorded as evidence that a Group A char-grilling tradition exists, explicitly not as a temperature
datum for arctic char. Group C returned nothing citable for this species in any method, and the queries are
in the ledger.

**The sweep changed once, because the cut exposed a gap in it.** After the leaves were written,
`arctic_char` derived clean — all five methods linked, ledger snapshot in agreement — yet its own ledger
listed seven open gaps. A queue reporting "no gap" for a cut whose prose says otherwise is exactly the
contradiction the new system exists to prevent, so the sweep now parses the ledger's status column and
annotates such cuts in its report (`arctic_char (authored ledger: 5 open — …)`). Two parser faults surfaced
while doing it: hand-written ids with uppercase letters (`group_C_absent`) and ids with a parenthetical
qualifier (`source_shortfall` (smoker)) were both silently dropped, so a ledger could look two-thirds
smaller than it was. Both fixed.

**Data state.** Five leaves, 22 records, 19 naming an internal temperature, three that do not (two give only
an oven or smoke setting, one gives nothing), one unverified byline, one smoke-versus-safety conflict, and
Group A present only as a same-genus silent record. The registry needed no change: it already declared the
same five methods and `medium`.
