## Four cuts from the queue — two leaves, two ledgers, and one deferral (0.10.2.30)

Worked the top of the potential queue with the equal-tie rule ("among equals, the cheapest evidence first").

**Why the literal number one was skipped.** `flounder` topped the queue, and its first two source attempts
proved it the most expensive of the eight cuts tied at potential 8 rather than the cheapest: the
flounder-specific German recipe page returned no readable body, and the Swedish queries returned only
generic white-fish guidance. The tie-break sent this pass to the four cuts where sources publish numbers.
That choice is a judgement, not a measurement, and it is written here so a later pass can disagree with it
deliberately.

**Plaice — `plaice-oven_bake.md`, four records, four-source gate met.** Tina Nordström (225 °C, ~15 min,
**48 °C at the bone**), Tasteline's editorial rule (**50–52 °C**, "går utmärkt att grilla, steka eller
ugnsbaka"), Allt om Mat / Annika Broman (sear 2 min a side then hold in an **80 °C** oven, no temperature
published) and Madens Verden / Holger Rørby Madsen (210 °C fan, fixed 20 min, no temperature). The useful
finding is structural: every record that names a technique rather than a number **slits the dark skin along
the backbone first**, because a curled flatfish lifts its thickest part off the tray. The gap is that all
four records are Nordic, and that the hub's `well_done` (63 °C) is unsupported by anything in the leaf.

**Monkfish — `monkfish-oven_roast.md`, four records, gate met.** Köket.se / Martin Persson (**50 °C in a
50 °C oven**), Mårtenssons Kök (20-minute 10 % brine, sesame crust, 3 min a side, then 150 °C to **52 °C**),
Lagat & Klart / Andreas Domeij (salt bed at 100 °C, **46 °C with a stated 45–50 °C range**) and godfisk.nu
citing Svenskfisk (**47–58 °C** for monkfish fillet, +4 °C for whole fish). Ovens span 50–150 °C while the
target barely moves, which is the leaf's finding: **the oven is a speed control, not a doneness control** for
this fish. Same gap as plaice — four Nordic records, and a `well_done` recommendation that every record
contradicts.

**Pike and pollock — research recorded, leaves deliberately withheld.** Both were researched and both fell
short of the four-record minimum, so instead of writing two under-evidenced leaves the verified findings went
into each hub's `## Data state and gaps` table with their URLs, numbers and the exact fetch failures:

- `pike`: johansmat.se (whole 1 kg fish in foil, **175 °C ~45 min to 54 °C at the bone**) and Tasteline's
  folie method (**275 °C**, cooking time computed from the fish's back width: 30 min for 4 cm, +5 min/cm) —
  plus two pages whose bodies could not be read (kokaihop.se states 52 °C, koket.se 225 °C).
- `pollock`: 56kilo.se (brined fillet, **170 °C to 46–48 °C, 8–12 min**) and the godfisk/Svenskfisk reference
  (**~52 °C** for white fish including pollock) — plus four pages with unreadable bodies (hemmakoket 50 °C,
  sjofolket 48–50 °C, ICA 52 °C, alltags-rezepte).

Those ledgers also record that three of the four pollock failures already state their number in the search
snippet, so the next pass can close that leaf with two fetches rather than a fresh search.

**A verdict on the leaves' own advice.** Both new leaves state, in their "Data applied" sections, that the
cuts' `well_done` recommendation at 63 °C is contradicted by every record collected and is left as an open
question in the ledger rather than changed silently. That is now three cuts in a row (arctic char, plaice,
monkfish) where the hub's doneness default sits above anything the corpus publishes — a pattern worth a
deliberate decision rather than another leaf-by-leaf note.

**Sweep state after the pass.** 187 cuts, 6 authored ledgers, 0 conflicts, 89 cuts with concrete gaps.
Plaice and monkfish stay in the queue: each still declares grill, pan-fry and smoker (and monkfish pan-sear
and braise) without data.

## The cut metric is renamed to improvement potential (0.10.2.32)

Step one of a longer, deliberately slow patch of the rubric: **the name only**. No number in any cut file
was recomputed, no criterion was added, removed or reweighted, and no deduction changed value.

**What the number means now.** The 0–20 count is unchanged in arithmetic — it is still 20 minus the
deductions — but it is no longer read as a score. It is read as **improvement potential**: **20 means
nothing left has been found to earn, and every point below 20 is potential, i.e. work still to do.** A file
at 8/20 therefore carries *more* potential than a file at 18/20, and a deduction lowers the number, which
raises the potential. That sentence, in slightly different words, now sits at the head of
`IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md` and at the head of the rubric in `RECIPE_COLLECTION_TOR.md`, because it is the one
thing a reader can get backwards.

**Where the name changed.**

- Frontmatter field `quality_score:` → `improvement_potential:` (187 cut hubs; nothing in the Python, the
  panel generator or the panel data reads or displays this field, so it is documentation-only).
- Section heading `## Quality score` → `## Improvement potential` (187 hubs + the TOR example block).
- Number label `**Score: N / 20**` → `**Improvement potential: N / 20**`, and the footer
  *"Score is recalculated…"* → *"Improvement potential is recalculated…"*.
- `RECIPE_COLLECTION_TOR.md`: section `## Cut file quality scoring` → `## Cut file improvement potential`,
  "Scoring summary table" → "Deduction summary table", "How the score is written" → "How the number is
  written", plus the purpose, exception and recurring-task paragraphs reworded from *score* to *number* /
  *number inflation*. The rules themselves are untouched: low numbers are the work queue, harsh readings are
  correct, the hard-wall exception stays the only sanctioned way past a deduction.
- `HOW_I_INFLATE_CUT_FILES_SCORES_AND_WHY.md` → **`HOW_I_INFLATE_CUT_FILES_AND_WHY.md`**, title "How I
  Inflate Cut Files — and Why". The ten patterns keep their content; only the noun changed, and Pattern 7/9/10
  are retitled from *score* to *the number*.
- `IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md`: new opening section defining the term and its direction; the task line now reads
  "the 8 cut files with the most improvement potential", and the "not a race to get high scores" paragraph
  now says "not a race to get a high number", "seeing 20 as doneness is wrong … update the rubric".
- User guide: one sentence, "the improvement-potential rules for cut overview files".

**What the sweep deliberately left alone.** About 70 occurrences of *score/scored/scoring* inside recipe
bodies are the **knife cut** — "score the fat cap", "deeply scored rind", "scored skin" — and were not
touched. `quality_score` in `recipes/models.py`, `recipes/matcher.py`, `api.py` and `panel_data.py` is a
different metric entirely (a 0.0–1.0 recipe-match quality), and was not touched. `quality_assessed:` is
still the date of the assessment and keeps its name. "Marbling score" in `bison_steak.md` is beef grading.

## Direction stated, and the four-leaf rule demoted to a floor (0.10.2.33)

Second patch, again **wording only**: no deduction was added, removed or reweighted, and no hub number was
recomputed. Two things were wrong with what 0.10.2.32 left behind.

**1. The direction was implied but not said.** The definitions said "20 means nothing left … every point
below 20 is potential", which is correct and easy to misread in a hurry. Both now state it the short way:
**the potential falls as the number rises** — one reading taken from opposite ends, the two never moving the
same way.

**2. "A target of 4" was reading as "4 is enough".** `IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md` said "the target is an average
of 4 cut-method files per cut file", and the TOR rubric said nothing at all about a leaf count except
`−3` when *no* leaf exists. Four was therefore both a goal and, effectively, a ceiling. Rewritten:

- **"Four cut-method files is rock bottom, not a target."** Four leaves on a four-method cut clears the
  minimum the system accepts and finished nothing; a cut with several traditions behind it often supports
  five, six or eight methods, and each of those is a leaf owed.
- **"Do not stop at four because four is the number in this file.** Four is where the minimum ends and the
  research begins." (TOR, method sub-files.)
- **Discovery now names what it owes.** Finding a method means registering it (step c) and writing its leaf
  in the same pass; finding that the animal or vegetable has a cut the tree does not contain means that cut
  gets its own cut file and its own leaves (TOR Step 2b). "A cut that is missing from the tree is potential
  the project has not even counted yet."
- **"The rubric never charges you for a method you name"** — the rows fire on missing evidence, not on work
  that has been found; the leaf is what turns a found method into evidence. Leaving a confirmed method off
  `methods:` is stated for what it is: a false statement about the cut, and the one defect the number
  cannot see.
- Comprehension example in `IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md` now says "four being the floor, not the goal", and
  `ground_moose.md`'s table row says "four-leaf floor met, not a ceiling".

**Measured while patching, not changed:** 58 of the 187 cut hubs currently declare 109 methods that have no
leaf file (worst: `flounder`, `pike`, `pollock`, `redfish`, `gilt_head_bream`, each with four declared methods
still un-leafed). Under the
rubric as written the number only sees *no leaves at all* (`−3`), so those 58 hubs show less potential than
they hold. Making the floor visible in the arithmetic would be one line — `−1` per declared method without a
leaf — but that is a scoring change, not a wording change, and it would require recounting those 58 hubs. It
is left for the user to call.

## The mark-scheme vocabulary follows the name out (0.10.2.34)

Third patch, and the answer to a fair question: after 0.10.2.33 the word **score** was gone from every
instruction file — it survived in exactly two deliberate contrasts ("improvement potential, not a score") and
one false positive ("underscores") — but the **mark-scheme vocabulary** was not. That is what made a change to
the arithmetic read as "a scoring change": the documents still described a graded exam, so thinking in marks
was the natural next step.

**What was there, and what it says now.**

| Was | Now | Where |
|---|---|---|
| "The purpose of this **rubric**" / "the rubric has always produced" | "this **table**" / "the **criteria** have always produced" | TOR, IMPROVE, inflation doc (8 sites) |
| "**Grading** cut files is a recurring improvement task" | "**Reading** a cut file is a recurring improvement task" | TOR |
| "### **Deduction** summary table", "Criterion \| **Possible deduction**" | "### The gaps, and the points they represent", "Criterion \| **Points**" | TOR |
| "**Maximum total deduction**" | "**Most a file can fall short of 20**" | TOR |
| "\| Criterion \| **Deduction** \|" (131 hubs), "**Deductions**:", "**Deduction**:" | "\| Criterion \| **Points** \|", "**Points**:" | all cut hubs |
| "**Total deduction −12 → 8/20**", "receiving **0 deduction**" | "**Total −12 → 8/20**", "receiving **0 points**" | hubs |
| "the deduction scale", "hub's deduction table", "no deduction per TOR", "partial-leaf deductions", "−1 overlap deduction" | "the scale", "hub's table", "no points per TOR", "partial-leaf gaps", "−1 overlap point" | hubs |
| "The **deduction** for 'no temperature evidence' is applied deliberately", "Deductions applied pending verification", "Group A deduction applies", "not a deduction under the criteria", "not a cut-level deduction" | "The **point** for …", "Points applied pending verification", "Group A point applies", "not a point under the criteria", "not a cut-level point" | 12 hubs |

**One sentence does most of the work**, and it is now in both instruction files. IMPROVE:
"Every minus figure in a cut file … is a point the file has **not earned**, which is to say a point of
potential. There is no penalty and no mark; there is only the list of what is still owed." TOR:
"**Read each −N as N points the file has not earned — N points of potential, never a penalty.**"

**Left alone on purpose.** Six uses of "deduction" in `HOW_I_INFLATE_CUT_FILES_AND_WHY.md` are inside quoted
notes an agent actually wrote at the time ("0 deduction", "deduction accepted as geographic reality"); they
are evidence, not instruction, and are quoted verbatim. Code-side `quality_score` (recipe matching) is
untouched as before. No number in any cut file was recomputed.

**Noticed, not fixed:** `beef/steaks/t_bone.md` carries an agent's mid-sentence aside in its points line —
"= −11 → Hmm that's 9 not 10. Wait, let me recount original points:" — which is a data-integrity defect in that
file, not a wording one. It is recorded here so it is not lost.

### Output 4 — My understanding of `IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md`

The task is to work three cut files — the three with the lowest improvement potential number, i.e. the most
left to do — and for each one to research the cooking methods the cut actually has across culinary traditions
and write the method leaves those methods are owed, with four to six fetched sources each; four leaves is the
floor, not a finish line. "This is not a race" means the number is not my target: I read it afterwards to see
what is left, I expect it to fall when research finds new methods, and I do not stop at four leaves, do not
guess a name or a temperature, and do not pad a leaf to reach a count. After all three cuts are done — not
before — I do CHORES.md: version bump, branch history, and a check that no help anchor broke.

### Output 4 — Your understanding of this file (own words)

The primary task for this pass is one named cut: build the cut file for "Tjocka revben" (front pork
belly with rib bones, no skin) and then improve it the way IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md prescribes — actively
research how this cut is actually cooked across culinary traditions, not assuming any methods list is
complete, and create a child cut-method file for every confirmed method (four leaves would be rock
bottom, not a target; a cut with several traditions behind it often supports more). "This is not a
race" means quality decides when the work is done, not time: each source recipe must be fetched and
read for real, verified human-created, from distinct culinary traditions, and padding, guessing, or
batching sources without reading them is worse than a smaller honest result. After the cut-file work
is complete — cut file, all owed leaves, updated `methods:` — I rate the cut under
`IMPROVEMENT_POTENTIAL_SYSTEM.md` and record the reading, then do CHORES.md.

### Method and cut inventory for `tjocka_revben` (collection ToR, Step 4 — written before any leaf)

**Cut identity (fetched, never guessed):**
- Svenskt Kött ( Swedish meat board ) cut page "Tjocka revben": front rib cut, meaty, traditional
  methods listed on the page itself: grill, oven, boil (förkok), smoke, sous-vide; internal
  temperature target **70 °C**; can substitute *bogblad* (blade); 250 g/person; often sold "knäckta"
  (cracked between bones).
- SV Wikipedia "Revbensspjäll": front ribs become *tjocka revben* (more attached meat); rear ribs
  become *tunna revben*, "även kallade spare ribs". So Swedish retail "spare ribs" = the THIN rear
  ribs, NOT this cut.
- Grilltips.se (Conny Arvidsson): US "full rack" butchery includes tjocka revben; Swedish racks are
  cut close to the bone to save *sidfläsk*.
- EN Wikipedia "Spare ribs": belly-side of the rib cage above sternum, meat and fat cover the bones;
  Chinese *paigu*, Korean grilled/stewed, Mexican adobo. US "spare ribs" is the closest non-Swedish
  identity to tjocka revben; US St. Louis cut = spare rib rack with sternum/tips removed.

**Methods already in cooking_data.py for sibling cuts** (baby_back_ribs, spare_ribs,
st_louis_ribs): oven_roast, grill, smoker, air_fryer.

**Methods researched for tjocka_revben this session (inventory):**

| Method | Real recipes found? | Sources fetched this session |
|---|---|---|
| oven_roast | Yes — very strong (SE/DE/AU/RU) | 4 full |
| grill | Yes (SE/FI/ZA/UK) | 4 full |
| braise | Yes (CN/IT/MX/PL) | 4 full |
| smoker | Yes (SE/AU/US) — only 3 traditions found | 3 full |
| slow_cooker | Yes (UK/US) — 2 traditions found | 2 |
| sous_vide | Yes — Svenskt Kött lists sous-vide for this cut; recipes found on Anova (CN-dim-sum style, JC) | 2 full + ChefSteps methodology page (JS-rendered, full steps not retrievable) |
| pressure_cooker | Yes in principle (pinaenlacocina notes a pressure-cooker variant of a spare-rib braise; koreanbapsang has none for this cut) — **collection not started; owed** | 0 |
| air_fryer | Not confirmed for this cut this session — sibling cuts have leaves; **owed** | 0 |
| steam | Chinese steamed *paigu* exists (woks of life "Steamed Pork Ribs with Red Fermented Bean Curd") — **collection not started; owed** | 0 |
| pan_fry / pan_sear | Chinese fried spareribs (woks of life) exist as riblet pieces — **not confirmed for the slab cut; owed** | 0 |

**Sources rejected this session for cut-identity failure (recorded, not used):**
koket.se "Southern style spare ribs" and "Rökta ribs" (both specify *tunna revben* / *sidor* —
rear ribs); koreanbapsang dwaeji galbi and slow-cooker galbijjim (both specify baby back ribs);
taste.co.za "Sticky braaied pork ribs" and "Sticky pot-braaied ribs" (pork LOIN ribs);
pinaenlacocina chile ancho costillas (boneless country-style = shoulder); soppa365 chimichurri
ribs (babyriibit); Anova "Fall-Off-The-Bone" (baby back).

## Instruction restructure — 2026-11-01 (commit 0c199593, no version bump)

Between the cut-creation pass and the 2026-11-01 sessions the cut-file instructions were split
into three files, and the change was committed without a timeline entry. Recorded here so the
branch history is complete:

- **`IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md`** went from carrying the whole flow to being a **hub** (~87 lines
  removed) that delegates: research follows `CUT_FILE_RESEARCH_TOR.md`, rating follows
  `IMPROVEMENT_POTENTIAL_SYSTEM.md`.
- **`CUT_FILE_RESEARCH_TOR.md`** (new) holds the session-start gate (the four verbatim outputs
  before any cut file is touched) and the per-cut sub-steps: method discovery, leaf creation,
  the GUI-visible-description requirement, and the instruction to rate only after the work is
  done and committed.
- **`IMPROVEMENT_POTENTIAL_SYSTEM.md`** (new) defines the reading that replaced the retired
  0–20 score. The scale runs the opposite way — **meat base 3455, fish and poultry 3350,
  anything else 3282**, reductions subtracted per type and per leaf (threshold 8/4/2,
  confirmed by the user on 2026-11-01), negative readings allowed, no reading means the cut
  is permanently finished. The reading only orders the queue; it never shapes the work.
- **`RECIPE_COLLECTION_TOR.md`** was tightened to match the split (~202 lines reworked),
  and **`HOW_I_INFLATE_CUT_FILES_AND_WHY.md`** gained a note that its worked examples belong
  to the old 0–20 scale and their arithmetic must not be reused.

Consequence visible in later sessions: old 0–20 scores and new readings are not comparable,
and every session-start gate since quotes `CUT_FILE_RESEARCH_TOR.md` rather than the hub.

### Analysis gate (verbatim)

Analysis begins only after the collection phase (4–6 fully saved source recipes
per leaf) is complete. Drawing conclusions from a leaf with incomplete source
recipes produces invalid data.

### Source integrity (verbatim)

The rule in this project is: **do not write content you cannot cite to a real URL or
a real published book**. Training-data recall of "what a recipe roughly contains" is
not an acceptable source. Use web_fetch to verify you can actually read the content.

### Understanding

The standing task is to research and improve the highest-potential cuts and their method leaves, but this session is specifically directed to tjocka revben. Research must be meticulous, traceable to real sources, and attentive to anatomy and cutting differences; hard cuts warrant further investigation rather than guesses or a race toward a score. After the requested cut work is complete, follow applicable chores and rate the work only afterwards.

### Method and anatomy inventory — 2026-11-01

Requested scope: revisit tjocka revben, not the general eight-cut queue. Registered methods before this pass: oven_roast, grill, braise, smoker, slow_cooker, sous_vide. New cut-exact evidence: slow cooker (Linda's Christmas ribs, Anders's carnitas); pressure cooker (Åse's 56kilo recipe). Pressure cooker is newly confirmed and requires a registered leaf. Other prior leads (steam, air fryer, fried riblets) have not yet been demonstrated for the exact front, meat-on cut in this pass and remain investigation, not asserted identities. Danish revelsben is rindless bone-in breast roast, a plausible anatomical comparator, not proven identical. German Brustspitzribs is a lower rib/breast cut, again a comparator, not a proved translation. No additional cut registration is claimed from these comparisons.

Sources read: https://extension.sdstate.edu/pork-carcass-fabrication-primal-and-retail-cuts ; https://grilltips.se/grilltips/kottguider/svenska-tunna-revben ; https://www.danishcrownprofessional.com/da-dk/produkter/bryst/revelsben-1-bens-i-skiver/ ; https://www.der-ludwig.de/grill-bbq/bbq-spareribs/spareribs-brustspitze/ ; https://lindasmathorna.se/juligaste-revbenspjallen-i-slowcookern/ ; https://ikoketmedanders.se/carnitas-pa-tjocka-revben-i-crock-pot/ ; https://www.56kilo.se/honung-och-ananasglaserade-tjocka-revben/ ; https://www.manilaspoon.com/korean-slow-cooker-pork-ribs/ ; https://thecarefreekitchen.com/slow-cooker-spare-ribs/ ; https://dadgotthis.com/sous-vide-spare-ribs/ ; https://voresmad.dk/opskrifter/gammeldags-revelsben-med-sauce-og-agurkesalat/ .

Do not count an anatomical comparison, recipe that specifies a different cut, time-temperature table or method-only guide as a cut-exact primary recipe. Pressure recipe itself conflicts: its introduction says 20 minutes before turbo; recipe card says 15 before turbo; report both, not a fabricated resolution.

### Revisit results — 2026-11-01

The 2026-10-31 session's completion claims were incorrect: the oven, grill and braise leaves had source-unspecified temperatures filled with a supposed house band and were nevertheless marked conforming. Their analysis sections and completion markers have been withdrawn; all seven leaves are incomplete under the strict gate. The earlier log is retained as history, not the current assessment. Several recipes describe ribs generally or St. Louis racks and cannot prove the Swedish front cutting line; they are retained as comparative research, not asserted exact matches. Linda's Christmas slow-cooker recipe and Åse's pressure-cooker recipe explicitly name tjocka revben; the latter's 15/20-minute setting conflict is recorded. The pressure-cooker method is registered and has its own incomplete leaf. The engine's recommended doneness key was corrected from `well_done` to `pulled` to match its sole configured `DONENESS_PULLED` range; the 70–80 °C Swedish guidance remains documented and does not imply the current preset supports that option.

Post-work rating 2503 is conservative: meat base 3455; cut 128; seven incomplete leaves 56; five cut-exact human recipes and their per-leaf group bonuses 320 (oven, grill, smoker, slow cooker, pressure cooker); two preferred-temperature schools 128; safe guidance 32; cut temperature behaviour in two languages 192; method/tradition survey 32; smoker and sous-vide method-temperature discussions 64. Broader comparative recipes are not credited as cut-exact; if their specific anatomy is verified later they can be reassessed. No leaf is credited as finished. Set `cooking_methods_researched` to 0 because the anatomy and methods survey is not yet complete.

Version bumped to 0.10.2.37, panel regenerated (PANEL_VERSION 682, 751 indexed files). No guide headings or help anchors changed.

### Non-English equivalents — 2026-11-01

The US comparison was the wrong first place to look, because US butchery has no named item for this piece; the meat and bone are distributed among shoulder, belly and loin. The German-language scheme does have one: item 5 "Brust(spitze) / Dicke Rippe", with Schweinebauch as a separate item 9 (de.wikipedia, Teilstücke des Schweinefleischs), described by a Bavarian butcher-meister as the *Dicke Rippe* with more connective tissue and cartilage. Austria calls the same item *Schweinebrust*. Denmark sells the zone as *revelsben*, cross-sawn one-bone slices of rindless bryststeg (Danish Crown Professional, 26.8 g fat/100 g, 180 °C ~60 min). Finland's BBQ literature separates *luinen kylki* from ribs — same meat as spare ribs but more left on the bone, longer cook (Poppamies). Polish fabrication yields *żeberka paski* from a narrow-cut belly and *żeberka grube* from the forequarter, and a culinary textbook defines *żeberka* as long thin thoracic rib meat with cartilage (mieso.com.pl; zpe.gov.pl). Norwegian *ribbe* is only recorded as side meat generally, so it is not treated as a match.

None of these sources asserts identity with the Swedish cut, and that caveat is written into the cut file. The practical gain is a better research target list: German, Austrian, Danish, Finnish and Polish rib-end/breast recipes instead of English spare-rib recipes.

No panel regeneration was needed — frontmatter and the indexed fields did not change; the edit is body text only.

### Asian, African, Caribbean and South American equivalents — 2026-11-01

Extending the language search outside Europe split the world cleanly. Chinese names the piece and cooks it constantly: 排骨 is an umbrella, but cutting a rack front to back yields 猪前排 and 猪小排, the latter described as front ribs with spine off, hard sternum kept, "肉层比较厚，并带有白色软骨" (Xinhua reprinting 科普中国; meishichina). Japanese names it by position instead — 肩スペアリブ, "肉に厚みがあり" (HyLife Pork), the shoulder-end rib that most Japanese supermarket spare ribs are, against the leaner バックリブ (Nippon Ham). Korean divides by rib number: front 갈비 #1–#5, 등갈비 as baby back, 마구리 as tips (Kimchimari), with 쪽갈비 described as the belly-adjacent lower rib (Naver blog, blog-level and with a 5-or-6 ambiguity). South Africa names a front "Thick Rib / Dikrib" distinct from Lies (belly) and Bors (breast), though it sells it as chops (southafrica.co.za quoting the SAPPO guide).

Elsewhere the piece is unnamed. Vietnamese has a thick-meat/bony axis (sườn non vs sườn già) but states no position and equates sườn non with rib tips. Thai's premium rib is the spine-adjacent one, the reverse polarity. Indonesian iga babi is a stub; Filipino terminology could not be verified at all and nothing was claimed. Nigeria, Ghana, Kenya and Zimbabwe use English "ribs"; Angola and Mozambique say entrecosto. Brazilian costela is generic, and the "mais carnudo" rib there is the belly one, costela de barriga (Seara). Spanish costilla is generic; Colombia's cut manual lists "Costilla de pecho" and "Costilla campesina" but only the contents page was readable, so those definitions stay unverified. Jamaica follows the British primal scheme with "spare ribs" only.

Consequence recorded in the cut file: Chinese is now the strongest research lead for this cut — named, meat-heavy, bone-in, and cooked braised/steamed/fried in its own right — rather than American spare ribs.

No panel regeneration needed; the indexed frontmatter did not change.

### Output 4 — Understanding of `CUT_FILE_RESEARCH_TOR.md`

My task is to improve the eight cut files with the highest improvement potential, one at a time, by researching the cut across culinary traditions with real, fetched sources and by giving every confirmed cooking method its own leaf. This is not a race: quality of the saved recipes and honest marking of what a source does and does not state matter far more than how many files I touch, so I will spend the time each leaf needs rather than declare a leaf finished to move on. Group A and Group C coverage is the concrete gap in this cut's leaves, and closing it requires found recipes, never guessed ones. After the eight cut files are done, and only then, I do `CHORES.md`.

### Method inventory for this pass (Step 4)

- **Already in `cooking_data.py`**: oven_roast, grill, braise, smoker, slow_cooker, sous_vide, pressure_cooker (7 methods, all with leaves).
- **Sources per leaf before this pass**: oven_roast 4 (groups B, D), grill 4 (B, C, D), braise 4 (A, B, D), smoker 3 (B, D). Groups A and C are thin or absent in most leaves.
- **Targets researched this pass**: Culinary Group A (Chinese 小排/前排, Japanese 肩スペアリブ, Korean 쪽갈비, Vietnamese, Thai) and Group C (South African, Nigerian, Caribbean, Levantine), because the naming research showed those are where a separate front-rib cut identity exists.
- **Methods to re-test for existence**: steam (蒸排骨 / 粉蒸肉 style), pressure cooking (高压锅), frying/deep-frying (cross-cut riblets), and air frying — registered only if a real recipe with a numeric endpoint is found.

### Regional recipe pass — 2026-11-01

Ran the `IMPROVE_CUT_FILES.md` flow again on `tjocka_revben` with the naming research as the search key, aiming at the four culinary groups rather than at the cut's European neighbours. The gate above was written first.

**What the search actually found.** Group A is rich in *named* cuts but poor in numbers: Cookpad Japan 24935187 is the only Japanese rib recipe that states a core temperature (near-bone 71 °C is done; the author's bake measured 80 °C), two Korean blogs repeat the 75 °C national minimum, and a Thai grilled-rib recipe states 165 °F. Chinese, Japanese and Korean home sites otherwise give oven settings and times. Group C is thinner still: of twelve African pages read, none gave an internal temperature; the Caribbean and Levantine material gave four (Kevin Is Cooking jerk at 195 °F, Hark BBQ at 165 °F then 195–205 °F, Pork.org's odd 145–195 °F range, and a Trinidadian reheat note that is not a doneness figure).

**What was written into the leaves.** Oven Roast gained a Japanese source (A) and a South African one (C) — now A/B/C/D. Grill gained Thai (A) — A/B/C/D. Braise gained Kenyan (C) — A/B/C/D. Smoke gained Indian (A) and Levantine (C) — A/B/C/D. Slow Cooker gained Chinese-tradition ribs (A) — A/B/D, C still missing. Pressure Cooker gained Japanese (A) and Levantine (C) — A/C/D, B still missing. Sous Vide stayed at two sources (A, B) with the Group C search recorded as empty; Japanese and Korean sous-vide rib material exists only as forum and video posts.

**Rejected, not stretched.** Savory Thoughts' slow-cooker jerk ribs are written for beef ribs and only note that pork works — a substitution, so not stored. Woolworths TASTE's "sticky braaied pork ribs" is a pre-sauced product instruction, not a recipe for a cut. Jamaica's butchery scheme has no front-rib name at all.

**Cut file.** Added a "Names for this cut in other languages" reference table covering all four groups with a status column (this cut / named comparator / generic / not a match / unverified), so future passes start from the index instead of re-deriving it, and added the coverage summary and the honest count of numeric endpoints.

**Reading not recomputed.** The system file specifies reduction values and says thresholds apply, but the threshold values themselves are not stated in it, so a recomputation would be invention. The recorded reading stays 2503 and no leaf became finished in this pass.

### Research-instruction fixes — 2026-11-01

Three gaps in the research instructions, found while reviewing how the regional pass had searched.

1. **Which language to search in.** Nothing in the instructions said, so English queries were quietly returning westernised, diaspora-facing recipes for Group A/C/D traditions — and the same failure one level down, where a regional cuisine searched only in the national language returns the cosmopolitan version. Added `RECIPE_COLLECTION_TOR.md`, `## Search in the language the cuisine is written in`: decide the language first, translate the cut and method terms from a real lookup, check the fetched page is actually in that language, treat English results for a non-English tradition as a wrong query rather than a result, log language and terms in the leaf's search log, and never claim a group is absent after searching only in English (that is what separates wall evidence from assumption). Cross-referenced from `CUT_FILE_RESEARCH_TOR.md` step 1a and two places in `SOURCE_SURVEY.md`, and added as **Pattern 11** in `HOW_I_INFLATE_CUT_FILES_AND_WHY.md` so it also stands as a trap: a group marked covered on diaspora sources, plus false absence claims.
2. **Cross-language cut names.** The language index first written into `tjocka_revben.md` now has a home in the instructions: `RECIPE_COLLECTION_TOR.md`, `## Names for this cut in other languages` — table columns, a closed status vocabulary (`this cut`, `named comparator`, `comparator`, `generic only`, `reverse polarity`, `not a match`, `unverified`), evidence kept in the prose beneath the index, and a leaf never asserting an equivalence the cut file does not carry. Also required when a new cut file is created (Step 2b).
3. **Descriptions before research.** `generate_frontend_data.py` lifts `## Cut profile` / `## Styckesprofil` from both cut files and leaves into `CUT_PROFILES` / `CUT_METHOD_PROFILES` / the `*_SV` equivalents, i.e. directly into the panel. Descriptions are therefore now written at file creation, before collection, with no placeholder and no copied sibling text: `Mandatory collection sections` (leaf), Step 2b (new cut file), cut-file ToR step 1b, and a checklist line.

The improvement-potential system was not touched. Android asset mirror refreshed for the four edited instruction files (that directory is gitignored).

### Gate decision and threshold decision — 2026-11-01

**Gate (user-approved).** The gate as written demanded a bolded numeric pull *and* numeric final serving temperature from every saved recipe, which no tjocka revben leaf can satisfy without inventing numbers. Measured across the seven leaves before the decision: oven roast 2 numeric of 6, grill 1 of 5, braise 0 of 5, smoker 4 of 5, slow cooker 0 of 3, pressure cooker 0 of 3, sous vide 2 of 2. The user chose the split rule, and it is now written in: non-numeric sources are saved in full and marked `**Pull temperature: not stated by source.**`, count normally toward the 4–6 collection requirement and toward source diversity, are never converted or estimated into a number, and are excluded from the range. `## Temperature consensus` opens at **3 numeric sources** per leaf, with the non-numeric sources listed beneath the table in a `### Non-numeric comparators` block in the source's own words. Implemented in `RECIPE_ANALYSIS_TOR.md` (Purpose, Verification Steps 2–4, analysis checklist, forbidden table) and in the matching collection-format rules in `RECIPE_COLLECTION_TOR.md` (pull/final-temperature step wording and the collection checklist). The two sentences the session-start gate quotes verbatim are unchanged byte for byte — checked programmatically.

**Thresholds (user-confirmed).** `IMPROVEMENT_POTENTIAL_SYSTEM.md` tiers every reduction by a threshold but never names one. Its heading, "Base rating and thresholds: Meat: 3455/8 · Fish and Poultry: 3350/4 · Anything else: 3282/2", is the base **and** the threshold in one line; the user confirmed that reading — meat threshold 8, fish and poultry 4, everything else 2, applied separately per reduction type and per leaf as the file already instructs. The system file itself was not edited: it is closed to edits without specific instruction, and none was given for it.

### Reading recomputed under the confirmed thresholds — 2026-11-01

`tjocka_revben` was re-read with the meat base **3455** and the now-confirmed meat threshold **8**, applied separately per reduction type and per leaf as the system file instructs. Every reduction type's instance count stayed at or below 8, so no instance was halved or quartered — the tiering rule was checked, not assumed.

| Reduction applied | Count × value | Deduction | Why it was (or was not) taken |
|---|---|---|---|
| cut file exists | 1 × 128 | 128 | one tjocka_revben.md in the tree |
| finished leaves | 0 × 96 | 0 | no leaf has concluded research (no analysis sections) |
| created, not finished leaves | 7 × 8 | 56 | all seven leaves exist and are unfinished |
| recipes in cut file | 0 × 64 | 0 | the cut file carries no saved source recipes |
| groups in cut file recipes | 0 × 32 | 0 | no cut-file recipes to carry groups |
| leaf recipes | 29 × 32 | 928 | oven 6, grill 5, braise 5, smoke 5, slow 3, pressure 3, sous 2 |
| leaf group instances | 24 × 32 | 768 | 4+4+4+4 A/B/C/D, slow 3, pressure 3, sous 2 |
| preferred temps of cut | 1 × 64 | 64 | verified paragraph: 70/80/90-95 C Swedish endpoints, cited |
| safe temps of cut | 1 × 32 | 32 | 63 C/145 F discussed in the profile with attribution |
| behaviour/fat rendering at temps, cut | 2 × 96 | 192 | English paragraph + Swedish Styckesprofil, once per language |
| behaviour/fat rendering at temps, leaf | 0 × 32 | 0 | no leaf profile discusses behaviour at temperatures; analysis sections withheld |
| method + traditions description, leaf | 3 × 32 | 96 | smoker, sous vide, slow cooker name the traditions; other four name one or none |
| method-specific preferred temps, leaf | 1 × 32 | 32 | sous vide only (75 C/5 h and 65 C/36 h, with why) |

**Sum of reductions: 2296. 3455 − 2296 = 1159.** Recorded in the cut file frontmatter and its `## Improvement potential` section; 2503 is preserved there as a superseded historical reading.

**Judgment calls, stated so they can be challenged.**
- *"Stub, but not yet finished"* is read as **created and unfinished**, so all seven leaves take 8 each rather than 0. Read instead as "skeleton files only", that line vanishes and the reading becomes **1215**.
- Leaf recipes and leaf groups are assessed **per leaf** (max 6 recipes, max 4 groups in any leaf), never pooled across the seven — pooling 29 recipes would have pushed that type past the threshold and halved it, which the file explicitly forbids.
- The cut's fat-rendering description is taken **twice**, English and Swedish, because the reduction says "max once per language" and the cut file carries both a `## Cut profile` and a `## Styckesprofil`.
- The "method and the traditions it is common in" line is taken only for **smoker, sous vide and slow cooker** — the other four leaf profiles name one tradition or none. Read as "names at least one tradition", four more leaves qualify and the reading becomes **1287**.
- Leaf-level fat-rendering and method-specific preferred-temperature lines are largely **0** because those descriptions live in analysis sections, which are withheld.
- The previous 2503 equals exactly 128 + 56 + 768: it counted the cut file, the seven unfinished leaves and the group bonuses, and omitted the 928 of leaf recipes, the temperature and description reductions and the method-tradition lines. Under-deduction overstates potential, which is why the number falls rather than rises.

**Consequences (not steering, just the result).** `tjocka_revben` moves down the queue. Separately, the new gate changes what is workable: **Smoke now has 5 sources and 4 numeric endpoints**, so it clears both the collection count and the three-numeric analysis gate, while Braise, Slow Cooker and Pressure Cooker hold no numeric source at all. Pattern 4 applies — the agent that did this work also rated it, so every judgment call above is written out for review rather than treated as a measurement.

### Output 4 — My understanding of `CUT_FILE_RESEARCH_TOR.md` (own words)

The primary task is to work through the cut files with the most improvement potential and, for each one, actively research cooking methods and culinary traditions rather than trusting the auto-populated method list, then create or improve every child cut-method leaf that real recipes justify — four leaves being the floor, not the goal — while the reading is only ever computed afterwards to order the next file. "This is not a race" means quality buys the tokens it needs: hard cuts are the ones worth staying with, small honest improvements count, no number means finished, and guessing names, sources or temperatures corrupts both the data and the rating system itself. After step 1 is complete I do `CHORES.md` and nothing earlier. In this session specifically I will search in each cuisine's own language, write every cross-language cut name I find into the cut file's index, and treat the GUI-visible descriptions as first-class work.

Group coverage is A/B/C/D on four leaves; the honest gaps are Sous Vide (no Group C), Slow Cooker (no Group C), Steam (A only), Air Fryer (no C or D), Charcoal Grill (no D). No Brazilian or Colombian source counts toward A–D: no group definition includes South America.

### New methods registered

`steam`, `air_fryer` and `charcoal_grill` added to the cut file's `methods:` and to `cooking_data.py` for id 223 (`supported_methods`), and `cooking_methods_researched` set to 1. Charcoal Grill has its own leaf rather than folding into `grill` because the sources distinguish fuel: the `grill` leaf's sources name gas or an unspecified outdoor grill, everything on the new leaf names charcoal, briquettes or firewood. **Deep-fry/pan-fry was discovered but not registered** — the `CookingMethod` enum has no `deep_fry` value and stretching it onto `pan_fry` would misreport the method. It is recorded in the cut file as a system gap awaiting a decision.

### Sources rejected rather than stretched

- Afrikaans *dikrib* stovetop recipe (yourrecipeblog.com) — the page states no species and its own site files it under "Bees - Beef".
- Epoch Times Taiwan charcoal rib column (李敏楠) — real technique column but prints no ingredient quantities, so it fails the collection format.
- Foodle Club slow-cooker ribs — its recipe card labels the dish "American, British", so it does not fill Group C.
- Anova "Fall-Off-The-Bone Sous Vide Ribs" and Korean koreanbapsang slow-cooker ribs — both specify **baby back ribs**; excluded for cut identity.
- 下厨房 (xiachufang.com) recipes 107576884, 107206632, 104189358, 106880038 — verified as existing but all four returned the site's 滑动验证 sliding captcha; recorded, not cited.
- Pholfoodmafia Thai dim-sum steamed ribs — 404 at the published URL.

### In-language search terms recorded per leaf

Each new and extended leaf carries a `**Search conducted in the language of each cuisine**` block naming the exact terms used, so the next pass does not repeat the dead ends: 蒸排骨 / 猪前排 / 干蒸排骨 / 粉蒸排骨 (Chinese steam), スペアリブ 蒸し / せいろ (Japanese), ซี่โครงหมูนึ่งเต้าเจี้ยว (Thai), sườn hấp (Vietnamese), 空气炸锅排骨 / 酱香烤排骨 (Chinese air fryer), Heißluftfritteuse Spareribs (German), costelinha airfryer (pt-BR), 쪽갈비 에어프라이어 and 숯불 돼지갈비 (Korean), Dicke Rippe Kohle / Holzkohlebriketts / indirekt grillen (German charcoal), braai varkribbetjie (Afrikaans), sous vide revben / revbensspjäll (Swedish).

### Cut-name index updates in the cut file (verified this session)

- **Finnish *grillikylki*** — verified as the retail label for the same zone, sold bone-in ("luullinen"), boneless and skinless ("nahka poistettu"); *luinen kylki* is Poppamies' own descriptive term for "bone-in side, not ribs". Sources: fi.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grillikylki, snellman.fi, vallanmaukas.fi, lihajakalakauppa.fi, keittotaito.com (equates *grillikylki* with *paahtokylki* from the *kylki* section), poppamies.fi.
- **Chinese 软边前排** — verified as the soft-edged sub-type of 前排 with more and larger cartilage (sohu.com/a/490318264_120234568). 软骨前排 does not exist; no Chinese standard defines the 软边/硬边 split, it is a market term.
- **Korean 갈비 fat trim** — the legal standard (식약처 별표 2) defines 갈비 as ribs 1–4/5 "피하지방을 제거하여 정형한다" (trimmed of subcutaneous fat), so the Swedish cut's fat cover is absent by standard; 쪽갈비 has no legal name.
- **South African *Thick Rib* / *Dikrib*** — strengthened with ButcherSA (cut 1, neck end, separate from 7 Belly / *Lies* and 8 Breast / *Bors*) and the Afrikaans parallel page "aan die onderkant van die nek uitgesny".
- **Polish** — *żeberka grube* (= *żeberka trójkąty*) is attested as a forequarter item in mieso.com.pl; *mostek* is brisket/sternum, in the normative scheme absorbed into *boczek*, **not** a front-rib name (wieprzowinabeztajemnic.pl, zpe.gov.pl lists mostek only for beef/veal).
- Brazilian *costela* and Colombian *costilla* rows re-labelled as **outside groups A–D**, coverage only.

### Analysis written this pass

Two leaves cleared the 3-numeric gate and their analysis sections are written: **smoker** (range 88–96 °C across Swedish, American, Indian and Levantine sources; Australian kept as a non-numeric comparator) and **sous vide** (bath range 63–75 °C across Chinese-dim-sum, modernist, Swedish and German sources; no source states a serving temperature, and the bath range sits *below* the `DONENESS_PULLED` floor of 88 °C, so a `method_temperature_ranges` override of `{"sous_vide": [63, 75]}` is written into the leaf as an instruction but deliberately **not applied** — it changes what the panel shows and belongs to a code decision). Both leaves end with `✅ CONFORMS TO TOR`; the stale "COLLECTION INCOMPLETE" notices across the tree were rewritten to the gate as revised on 2026-11-01, and pressure cooker and oven roast were corrected to state their real status (collection complete, analysis withheld at 1/4 and 2/6 numeric).

| behaviour/fat rendering at temps, leaf | 2 × 32 | 64 | smoker and sous vide analysis sections discuss behaviour at temperature; the other eight have no analysis |
| method + traditions description, leaf | 6 × 32 | 192 | smoker, sous vide, slow cooker, steam, air fryer and charcoal grill profiles each name the traditions; the other four name one or none |
| method-specific preferred temps, leaf | 2 × 32 | 64 | sous vide (bath temps with why) and smoker (pull band with why), both from written analysis |

**Sum of reductions: 3488. 3455 − 3488 = −33.** The system file allows negative readings explicitly ("Negative readings are allowed; do not clamp them to zero"), so −33 is recorded in the cut file frontmatter and its `## Improvement potential` section, with 2503 and 1159 preserved as superseded historical readings.

**Judgment calls, stated so they can be challenged.**
- *Finished* is read as "has written analysis sections". Read as "all 4–6 sources collected and gates fully met", only smoker and sous vide qualify anyway, since every other leaf still misses the numeric gate.
- Leaf recipes and leaf groups remain assessed **per leaf** (max 6 recipes, max 4 groups), never pooled; the tiering rule was checked and no type exceeded the threshold of 8.
- "Method and the traditions it is common in" is now taken for **six** leaves — the three earlier ones plus steam, air fryer and charcoal grill, whose profiles each name their traditions explicitly. Read as "at least one tradition named", oven roast, grill and braise would also qualify and the reading would fall by 96 more (−129).
- "Behaviour/fat rendering at temperatures, leaf" and "method-specific preferred temperatures, leaf" are taken for smoker and sous vide only because those are the two leaves with analysis sections; the three new leaves describe *heat behaviour* in their profiles but state no preferred temperature, so they take the method-traditions line and not the preferred-temps line.

The Android asset mirror was refreshed for the edited leaves, the cut file and `cooking_data.py`'s directory (that directory is gitignored). `IMPROVEMENT_POTENTIAL_SYSTEM.md` was not edited — it is closed to edits without specific instruction.

## CHORES (0.10.4.0) — 2026-11-01

Run after the improvement potential system was clarified and fourteen cut files were re-rated, per CHORES.md.

### 1. Version

`0.10.3.0 → 0.10.4.0` (third digit +1, fourth reset) in `manifest.json`, `__init__.py` (`__version__` and the Last
Change header) and `const.py` (Last Change header). `PANEL_VERSION` stays at 686: `generate_frontend_data.py` does not
read `improvement_potential` or the `## Improvement potential` section, so the panel data is unchanged.

### 2. Branch history

**Spec clarifications restored.** Five rules had fallen out of the system text and were re-added, because the same
questions were being answered differently each time (commits `ab9e5a6d`, `cd6cde54`, `8b2808bb`):

- `Meat: 3455/8` is a base of 3455 with a threshold of 8, not a divisor.
- Thresholds tier per reduction type and **per cut-method leaf**, never against a pooled count.
- The "specific description of the cut-method and traditions" row is a **leaf** row, counted per leaf and per language.
- Each language present (English, Swedish, Danish) takes its own deduction.
- The behaviour/fat-render rows require an actual discussion of behaviour across more than one temperature, or an
  explicit statement that no temperature data exists.

**Re-rating from scratch.** The fourteen cut files converted in `87ad7d32` were re-rated under the locked rules; those
first readings were computed against the ambiguous text and were never released. The former 0–20 readings stay in each
file as historical values. Evidence used per file: leaf count, finished/stub classification from the collection and
analysis gates, recipe count per file, culinary-group letters per file, and for each descriptive row whether the text
actually reasons about temperature or about the method's traditions.

| Category | Cut | Former /20 | New |
|---|---|---|---|
| beef | `short_ribs` | 19 | **2287** |
| beef | `flat_iron` | 11 | **-33** |
| fish | `eel` | 20 | **870** |
| fish | `grayling` | 12 | **1838** |
| game | `moose_steak` | 9 | **2735** |
| game | `mutton_chop` | 9 | **2735** |
| lamb | `ground_lamb` | 8 | **2495** |
| lamb | `lamb_rump` | 9 | **2551** |
| pork | `pork_tenderloin` | 8 | **1423** |
| pork | `pork_leg` | 8 | **2143** |
| poultry | `chicken_burger` | 8 | **2494** |
| poultry | `ground_turkey` | 9 | **2102** |
| vegetables | `button_mushrooms` | 9 | **2066** |
| vegetables | `roasted_carrots` | 9 | **2306** |
